# draft — design reasoning

The reasoning behind the names and rules in `CLAUDE.md`: superseded designs, rejected
alternatives, the QA findings that produced each rule, and the measurements taken at the
time. Split out of `CLAUDE.md` on 2026-08-20, when that file had reached 27,000 words and
was being loaded in full into every session.

**`CLAUDE.md` is operative; this file is the record.** Where the two disagree, CLAUDE.md
wins — this is a verbatim snapshot of the reasoning as it stood on 2026-08-20 and is not
kept in step. Read it when a rule in CLAUDE.md seems arbitrary, or before re-proposing
something that may already have been tried and rejected.

Prune an entry once its reasoning is dead rather than merely old. Git holds the rest.

---

A group drafting engine: patches race, blind pairwise judgments rank them, adoption clears a rising confidence bar. SPEC.md is the source of truth for the mechanism; when spec and code disagree, the spec wins until Ed amends it.

## Documents

- `SPEC.md` — the mechanism spec, and the single source of truth. Amend only with Ed's sign-off; bump the version. No separate decision log: resolved decisions are folded into the spec ("keep latest design, not history" — Ed, 2026-08-13).
- `QUESTIONS.md` — open and deferred items only, numbered from one continuous project-wide sequence (Ed answers by number). Never renumber or reuse numbers; delete items once folded into the spec. Questions raised in chat that Ed doesn't answer before the session ends belong here with real sequence numbers — otherwise they die in the transcript. Draw in-chat numbers from this sequence too when they concern project decisions; ad-hoc chat numbering collides with it and makes the record ambiguous (learned 2026-08-15).
- `PRODUCTION.md` — the road to docs.vote: the staged rollout, the security-defect list, the Postgres-hybrid design, the erasure answer, the go-live checklist. A working document — stages get checked off as they land. Supersedes the old `PLAN.md` (deleted 2026-08-20; git history keeps it).
- `design/*.html` — the mockup series (race-card, session-view, document-creation, founding-ceremony): light-only, Bootstrap-plain, one continuous fictional world (the Hollow Oak Club charter). **They share one stylesheet, `design/system.css`** (2026-08-17), which is session-view's own, lifted out when Ed asked *can we import as much of the session-view as we can? I think ultimately this will literally be the session view.* The two setup surfaces share **two more files** on top of it (2026-08-18): `design/setup.css`, the small vocabulary a live surface has no use for (the setup band, the choice list, the consent slider, distributions, faces), and `design/setup.js`, the machinery — tab group, cable, rail entry, card shell, the read-only and watching bodies, avatars, the identity card. **Since 2026-08-18 all three surfaces also share `design/cards.js`** (see the glossary entry): the decision-card grammar itself, lifted out of session-view so the setup surfaces render cards with session-view's own code instead of imitating it — Ed's call, with the pre-extraction session-view frozen in `design/reference/` (tag `pre-cards`) and every change to cards.js gated by `design/tools/session-probe.js` proving the live page byte-identical to the frozen one. What is left in each surface is its own fixture and the bodies of the cards *it* may fill in, which is the only thing that actually differs between them. They are no longer *single* static files, and that is the point: two copies of one design system drift, and the housekeeping pass of the same day was largely an inventory of what drifting had already cost inside a single file. A surface adds only what a live one does not need. **Since 2026-08-18 (evening) the two setup surfaces are one file, `design/setup.html`** (Ed, 361: *merge now*) — `document-creation.html` and `founding-ceremony.html` are redirects; see the `setup.html` glossary entry.
- **Every surface is the session-view.** Setup, the founding ceremony and a live session are the same three columns, the same rails, the same card-over-document gesture — because the finding underneath is that **whatever the document wants from you goes in the right-hand rail, and everything that wants something is a card**. A setup question, a founding question and a judgment are the same object in the same place; only the modal-over-document differs from the card-in-place, and only because a setup question has no clause to open out of. Each carries its own design notes and named-parts glossary at the bottom — except `session-view`, whose notes moved to `design/session-view.notes.md` on 2026-08-15, and the two setup surfaces, whose shared task grammar and founding dependency tree live in `design/setup.notes.md` (2026-08-18, kept current at Ed's ask) (Ed, 76: nothing sits underneath the three-column view). Keep new surfaces consistent with the series.
- `packages/sim-harness/REPORT-deferred-evidence.md` — findings from the 2026-08-14 evidence pass (Q8/Q9/Q10/Q13), with reproduce instructions.
- `REVIEW-creation-session.md` — the 2026-08-18 end-to-end walk of creation → founding → live session, written against SPEC v0.30. **Not stale history: open Q335 points at its §7** as the definitive settings-to-engine field mapping, which is what the `Constitution` split needs. Keep it until that lands; its findings 338–344 are all folded and only the mapping is still load-bearing.
- `README.md` — the public repo's front door, and since docs.vote went live the first thing a stranger reads. It states the spec version, the four packages and the test counts, so it goes stale silently — check it whenever one of those changes.

## V1 product decisions

- Target context: constitutional conventions for Newspeak House cohorts. Rosters typically 5–10, conventions 15–20; design must not preclude 100+/1000+ instances, but v1 tunes to small rosters (data-efficiency over throughput).
- Hosted multi-tenant web service; magic-link auth against roster emails. Invitations send from **docs.vote** via **Resend** (Ed, 2026-08-18) — the sending domain was the real hosting decision, and the host follows from it.
- Documents are Markdown, rendered as rich text; usually a few pages, long-document behavior stays in scope for sim experiments.
- TypeScript end-to-end; engine-core is a pure, dependency-free library shared by sim-harness and product server.
- Sim personas are LLM-powered on a cheap model (Haiku-class) and speak the same participant API as human clients — no sim backdoor; mixed human/bot sessions are a goal.

- UI north star (Ed, 2026-08-14): **suggestion-mode with escalation** — the default surface reads like familiar Google-Docs-style inline suggestions with approval; the race view (overlay model, pairwise cards) is the escalation state that appears only where suggestions collide or stakes demand ceremony. Most of a session should feel like approving typo fixes; the machinery earns its visibility.

## The two kinds of decision

**This is a constitution editor, and the question is which decisions are ordinary and which are constitutional** (Ed, 2026-08-18). The test is his: **a constitutional decision is one that would make past decisions mean something different.** Disclosure, the chamber, quorum, the bar, the roster and whether the document ends at all are constitutional; the starting text, the title, the link, the closing date, the grant, the drip, the stake and AI proposals (Q352, Ed 2026-08-18: the auditor is not a member, so switching it re-rates nothing — *the tool is just a convenience for the membership*) are ordinary; a member's name and picture are neither. The line can fall **inside** one setting: the window is one question whose answers include *never*, so moving the closing date is ordinary and removing the ending is constitutional — the route belongs to what a motion changes rather than to what card it sits on (Q329). A `motion` is the act of proposing a change to any of them, and the route it takes is a fact about the *setting*: an ordinary motion is an ordinary race, judged at the bar; a constitutional one is **a unanimous vote on a proposed amendment** (Ed, 2026-08-18, superseding the earlier asked-again design): one refusal keeps what stands, and until everyone has answered, what stands stands. The founding itself still collects minima by construction (§9.0a) — unanimity is the one decision rule with no quorum question inside it to govern, which is what keeps the bootstrap shut at both ends. SPEC §9.6–9.7, v0.48.

**Three consequences worth keeping in mind.** *The test is time-indexed by its own wording* (Ed, 2026-08-18, §9.6a): before judging opens there are no past decisions for a change to re-rate, so **before the start nothing is amended — only set**. The convenor re-sets their settings and re-shapes the roster freely until the moment judging opens; a motion is the act of changing a *settled* document’s rules. With it: **membership begins at first arrival** — an invitation is an invitation *to become* a member, invitees are listed as *invited* and count toward nothing (which is what keeps one unopened email from blocking judging), and a roster change while ceremony answers are being collected is a **ground shift**: answers stand, their authors are notified and may revise. And **delegate the decision, not the field** (Ed, Q341: *rethink this given what is being decided*): a delegated question collects exactly the binding scalar — the bar **at the close**, the quorum **number** in the convenor’s chosen form (count or share, Ed’s 338) — while the machinery it rides on (the ramp’s shape and start, quorum’s form) is pacing or wording, stays with the convenor, and is **ordinary by the test**: every adoption is recorded against the bar as it stood, so re-shaping the ramp re-rates nothing. Which split ✒️ into two cards — *How sure must the room be?* (constitutional, the close) and 📈 *How does the bar get there?* (ordinary, pacing) — and made quorum one card with two eyebrows, *Asked as* (the convenor’s) and *The number* (the room’s). Quorum also stopped being fiction: F = max(Q, min(⌈E/3⌉, F_max)) in §4.2, the room’s number riding on the statistical minimum.

**Two consequences worth keeping in mind.** *Constitutional settings default to the roster and ordinary ones to the convenor*, because the room can take an ordinary one back cheaply and cannot take a constitutional one back at all. And *the opening question is the amendment question*, so amending a constitution needs no new object anywhere on the surface.

**The tab you click does not move**, and it took a QA to make it true (Ed, 2026-08-18: *why do my constitutional clause-tabs-piles jump around when I click on them?*). It was claimed here and false in the code, by 33px down and 14px right: a closed pile inherited `.chipcol`'s 14px gutter margin, which is the right distance from a **paragraph** and the wrong one from a **card edge**; and the open card's strip hangs off the `.headclause`, which starts below the card's own label, so the first tab arrived a label's height lower than the pile front it replaced. The margin is 0 for a closed pile — its right edge is exactly the card's left edge — and the vertical half is corrected in `fitBand` by **measurement** rather than by a constant, because the label wraps on a narrow card and the distance is not a number anybody can know in advance. Measured after: 0px in both axes.

**Two more rules the piles carry.** *A constitutional setting a member had no say in is a decision they are owed* (Ed, 2026-08-18): it sits in their rail until they open it and press **OK**, and then it leaves the rail and lives in the pile — the live rail's unacknowledged-decision rule arriving from the other end, because a rule you never chose and are bound by is exactly the thing a margin must not let scroll past. Reading is not enough, because reading is what you do to everything. The **ordinary** settings the convenor made never enter a member's rail at all: nothing is being asked, and anybody may motion one at the bar whenever they like. And *the pile headings are the people* — the convenor's name and picture under **Convenor**, the membership's under **Membership**, arriving as they do, with a placeholder before anybody has one. Which turns the band at the head of the document from a list of settings into the room itself.

## Names (Ed, 2026-08-18)

**founder** — whoever creates and administers a document (Ed, 2026-08-18: *it's time to change "convenor" to "founder". It's much more natural* — reversing the same morning's never-founder rule; never "admin" survives). "Convenor" persists in SPEC and engine code as the older term, like roster for membership. **member** — anybody on the roster; the word "roster" survives in code, but the surface says **membership** (Ed, 2026-08-18). A convenor who is not a member is a **clerk**, and a convenor who is one appears in the membership like anybody else. **Observers are not members** (Ed, 2026-08-18: *we don't collect any information about them, they just have the public URL*) — there is no observer role, no row, no name and no picture, because nothing about them is known. Who may read a document is settled entirely by the 🌍 **Visibility** setting (the engine's `chamber`; titled *Who Else Can See It?* until Ed renamed it, 2026-08-18 — a noun term of art like Quorum, glyph 🌍), and SPEC §9.3's observer role does not survive that (Q324). **Three words left the surface on 2026-08-18.** *The bar* became the **approval threshold** — a bar is a metaphor that has to be learnt, it reads as a height, and the copy then had to keep explaining that clearing it is not winning a vote; the literal name says what the number is for, and it is still a **confidence** rather than a vote share (§5), which the copy has to keep saying either way. *Economy* and *tokens* became the **proposal rate** — both described the machinery from outside, where the question a member is actually being asked is how often they may propose. And **participant** is the engine's word for whatever speaks the `participant-api` and should never appear in anything a member reads. **"Ordinary" left the surface too** (Ed, 2026-08-18): ✏️ already means a proposal, so the label said the machinery twice — the kind pair is glyphic on cards, **✏️** *anybody may propose changing it* and **🏛️** *a constitutional change, the ask-everyone route* (Ed, choosing it over 🤝 and 📢: *about time we used 🏛️ for something!*). On a card head the pair reads "🏛️ Constitutional · the members decide" / "✏️ Open to proposals · reserved by the convenor"; the word *constitutional* survives on the surface, "ordinary" survives only as engine vocabulary in SPEC and code. And **"ceremony" has left the surface entirely** (Ed, 2026-08-18: *something more neutral like "everyone is ready to begin"*) — it named a real stage and dressed it in robes, which is the wrong signal for a screen whose whole argument is that this is ordinary. What is left is what happens: everyone answers, and when everyone is ready the document begins.

## Glossary — named parts

Use these names in all discussion, commits, and code. Literal and stable beats elegant.

**Engine (mechanism, no UI):**
- `engine-core` — session state machine: candidates, races, adoptions, the adoption-threshold ramp, tokens/refunds, adoption floors, certification. **Caught up through 367(b)** (2026-08-19): quorum rides the floor (§4.2), the drip is real minutes (`tokenDripMinutes`, Q353), amendments bind races in flight (`amend`/`standing-set`, §4.3 anchor re-seating — a bar never jumps), suspension is lapse engine-side (§9.5a), **setting races** carry ordinary motions (Q390 — a candidate is a patch *or* a typed value; adoption of a value is a verdict, applied only by the host's `setStanding`, which is also the ground shift), and the hash is pure TS — the engine is node-free and browser-loadable. **Adoptions batch on the cooldown metronome** (Ed, 2026-08-19, closing Q399; SPEC §4.2 v0.58): when the cooldown from the last batch elapses, every race clearing bar+floor adopts at once — the document changes at most once per cooldown, by as much as the room decided; one decision per race per batch (the ready set snapshots before anything lands), oldest first, each rebasing the next; a race adopts only once somebody has actually judged in it (two rival authors meet a floor of 2 on derived self-preferences alone — the old one-race trigger enforced this structurally, the sweep says it explicitly). `Session.tick(t)` is the host clock; the bridge's `tick(t)` rides it (judge's act-harvest-sync pattern) and the server's minute timer drives the bridge, so a due batch lands even in a quiet room. The cooldown stays **engine tuning, never constitutional** (Ed: a convenience, part of the UI), and a cleared race is never surfaced as waiting — no ⏳, since “cleared and cooling” does not exist as a state and deriving it would be a standings reveal.
- `adoption-threshold` — the confidence bar a challenger's win-probability must clear; ramps over the session window (wall clock). Formerly "θ"; always use this name.
- `patch-engine` — text machinery: diffs, footprints, three-way merge, rebase, surgery.
- `overlap-gates` — the three-gate classifier for colliding patches: textual composition → semantic composition → rivalry.
- `ranking-model` — Bradley–Terry preference model (per race) + global salience model, with active pair sampling and deadlock detection.
- `router` — v/c_p feed ordering, hot set, exploration/salience streams, floors-aware serving, notification digests.
- `event-log` — append-only hash-chained log, seeded RNG, participant receipts.
- `dedup-gate` — submission-time duplicate check (embeddings, edit distance, LLM equivalence) plus behavioral dedup probes.
- `race-labeler` — advisory naming and typing of disputes: a name ("treasurer oversight") and a type (copy-edit · substantive · structural) per race, from the oracle's `describeRace`, with a deterministic nearest-heading + excerpt fallback. Outside the state machine — labels never enter the event log or gate anything; type is stored but not yet wired to routing (Q49).
- `document-modes` — two **independent** settings, both fixed at creation (SPEC §9.0). **Window**: an end datetime, or none (perpetual) — **one question with one answer**, which is why the surface asks it on one card (⏰ *When does it end?*) with *never* as an option rather than on two (Ed, 2026-08-18). **Threshold shape**: fixed, or ramping. A windowed document may hold its bar fixed; only a perpetual one is *constrained*, since a ramp needs an endpoint to ramp toward. A window gives you the closing ceremony and a drip paced against it; without one the drip runs against real time and the document ends by `freeze` instead.
- `founding-ceremony` — the stage before any drafting where the roster sets quorum and threshold: each member states, blind, the *lowest they will accept*, and the document takes the **maximum** of each. A consent rule rather than a vote, which is what lets it escape the constitutional bootstrap — there is no vote to govern, because the result satisfies every stated minimum by construction. Distribution published without names (SPEC §9.0a).
- `sign-out` — a member declaring they are done, choosing **holding** (they stay in the quorum base, so enough of them freezes the document — *I do not consent to you finishing without me*) or **abstaining** (they leave the base — *I trust you to finish up*). Plain silence is never sign-out; judgments already cast keep counting afterwards; the base is the whole roster minus abstainers, never the active remainder (SPEC §9.5).
- `freeze` — what happens when active plus holding falls below quorum: races park, participants are notified, the record is cut. A stall with an alarm, not a death — it thaws if enough return, and it is the perpetual mode's equivalent of a close.
- `coherence-auditor` — machine drafter patrolling document drift, fixed token budget. **Not a member** (Ed, 2026-08-18): it never judges and counts toward no quorum — it only proposes, and its proposals compete like anybody's. The surface card is **AI Proposals** 🤖 (was *Is There a Machine Member?*), and it lives in the **Proposals** section (the renamed Proposal Rate) beside the grant and the drip. **Ordinary since Q352** (Ed, 2026-08-18): it defaults to the founder, judging never waits on it, and a motion on it is a race — delegating it to the room stays the founder's option, most-restrictive answer winning.

**Design system (session-view, tokenised 2026-08-16 — the composer should adopt it rather than invent its own):**
- `cards.js` — **the decision-card grammar, one implementation** (2026-08-18, the system.css move again, after Ed's QA kept failing on setup cards for the structural reason that they were hand-written imitations of session-view's). `window.CARDS`: a pure layer (the diff/markdown engine, `resultOnly`, `speakerHtml`, `fieldHtml`, `fieldOf`, the drawn TICK/CROSS and MARK glyphs) and a `make(env)` factory (lane bars, proposal blocks, `clauseHeadHtml`, commit row, `laneBoxHtml`, the open/close geometry, `keepStill`) whose env keys are **functions read at call time** — session-view's `topUrgentId` is a reassigned `let`, its `chilled` a mutable Set, so nothing may be captured at make() time. Seams: `o.marks`/`o.washAttrs` on the clause head (the setup strip arrives pre-wrapped; session's chips/wash inject unchanged), `valAttr` (session radios speak `data-v`, setup composers `data-mval`, motion judging `data-motion`), a bindable speaker tooltip (setup carries the audited no-spec-citation copy). Load order on every consumer: `cards.js` → `setup.js` → inline. **The contamination guard**: `design/reference/` holds byte-frozen pre-extraction copies (tag `pre-cards`; never edit — re-freeze on intentional change), and `design/tools/session-probe.js` walks all 43 cards on the live page against the frozen one — whitespace-normalized outerHTML equality per card plus 0.0px geometry; it must be re-run **whenever cards.js changes**, and both runs must share one window size. The lift removed ~820 lines from session-view and was proven byte-identical. **The open/close animation clips only the bottom edge** (Ed's QA, 2026-08-19: *when I click on a tab, it disappears briefly before the decision-card appears*): `overflow: hidden` during expand/collapse was clipping the tab strip, which hangs entirely outside the card's box (`right: 100%`), for the whole motion — replaced with `clip-path: inset(-60px -100px 0 -100px)`, so the vertical reveal survives and the overhang stays visible; probe re-run IDENTICAL, 0 diffs (both pages probed from scroll 0 — a restored scroll position on one side reads as a constant offset in every rect). Two finds it forced: fitBand's head-rule correction had been **silently defeated by margin collapse** since the piles dissolved into block `.cpara`s (fixed with `display: flow-root` on the open paragraph — the discipline read 0.0px again only after being genuinely 20px off), and `motionCompose` plus the `.setrow` pile-fit pass were dead code the settled-card-is-the-composer build had already superseded.
- `wash` — the lifecycle colour on any surface that carries one: a clause, a `clause-head`, a held-open gap, a queue entry. **Colour changes fade** rather than jump, over `--wash-ms` (700ms), because a wash changing means something happened and a jump leaves the reader to notice it after the fact (Ed, 2026-08-17). Two things make that work: a queue entry's wash is painted as a **bar** (a `::before` whose width is the fill and whose background-color is the hue) rather than as a hard-stopped gradient, since no browser interpolates between two gradients; and because both rails are rebuilt wholesale on every render, each washed element is rendered wearing its **previous** colour and handed the new one after one forced reflow — keyed by what the wash is *about*, which is why a clause and the head of the card that swallows it share one key and opening a card deepens the colour rather than repainting it.
- `room-pulse` — one grey dot in the topbar that beats once per action by anybody, yourself included (Ed, 2026-08-17). The document is live and other people are working in it, and nothing on the surface said so. Deliberately **content-free**: no count, no direction, no author, so the only thing it can say is *the room is moving* — which is also what keeps it inside SPEC §3.5, since a heartbeat with nothing in it cannot leak standings. Grey at rest and grey at its brightest, because activity is information and nothing is being asked of you; it beats by growing a ring rather than by changing colour, since a colour change in the topbar would read as a state where this is an event. In the mockup the other fourteen members are a timer on a fixed *irregular* cycle — irregular because a metronome reads as a machine rather than as people, fixed because a mockup that behaves differently every time is one you cannot QA.
- `me` — the user avatar at the right of the topbar. Initials, not a photograph: there are no accounts behind it yet. It is the only face on the surface, since everybody else's authorship is sealed (SPEC §3.4) — Q278 asks whether that stays true.
- `lifecycle palette` — **three** hues held as raw RGB channels (Ed, 2026-08-17, third pass): `--lc-open` 🔥💡⚔️ yellow, `--lc-weigh` 🌶️ pink, `--lc-yours` ✏️ **blue**, and one grey shared by `--lc-deciding` ⏳ and `--lc-closed` ✔✖↻. Two moves made it one idea rather than six colours. **⏳ goes grey**: you have judged it and the race runs on without you, which is the purest piece of information on the surface and was wearing the accent — and it is the same thing ✅❎☑️ say, so they share a hue. **✏️ goes blue**, specifically the accent, because your own work is the one state about *you* rather than about the question, and the accent is already what this surface uses for you: your caret, your selection, the control you have turned on. Green leaves the margin entirely as a result, which is most of Q264 — `--ok` now means *decided* and nothing else. The greys are lighter than they were, on the same reasoning: a state that wants nothing should not be as loud as one that does. The rule this costs is 164's *colour means you can still affect it*, since a ⏳ race is revisable until it seals; the replacement is narrower and truer — **grey means nothing is being asked of you** (🔄 strains it and keeps grey anyway: what it tells you is that your old judgment is gone, not that it wants a new one yet). **🔥 gave up its own hue** (Ed, 2026-08-17): a flame is *an ordinary judgment that wants you most* — the same kind of thing as a bulb with a priority on it — so orange was claiming a difference in kind to say a difference in degree. It is the deepest yellow instead. Getting that to work took one measurement: dropped straight onto the urgency ramp it came out at alpha **0.292** against a neighbouring 💡 at **0.280**, which nobody can see, and which is not a fixture accident — the flame is usually only marginally the most urgent, because somebody has to be first among a set of close things. 🔥 does not mean *the top of the ramp*, it means **the question you are being asked next**, which is a category, and a ramp cannot express a category. So it takes a fixed alpha clear of the ramp's ceiling (`FLAME_A`, 0.44) and the ramp runs underneath it: same hue, decisively more of it. And **hot for actions, cold for information** (Ed, 2026-08-17) — orange and yellow are the states that want something from you, blue and grey are the ones telling you where things stand. That second rule is what placed the diagonal: it wants a judgment, so it must be hot; it is a different *kind* of judgment from a proposal, so it must not be the same hot as one. Applied identically to the queue card's wash, the paragraph's wash and the gutter mark. Green is available in a margin only because every *decided* state washes grey there — `--ok`'s green never leaves a card interior, and the two must not be allowed to meet (Q264).
- **Open is said by depth, not by outline** (Ed, 2026-08-17): no active box carries a border — not the decision card, not the drafting box, not the open rail entry. `--shadow-xl` is the only step allowed to be big, and it is still a light source rather than a halo (offset grows with blur, blur near 2.5x it, contact shadow keeping the near edge crisp). This retires 198’s one-blue-for-every-card as a *boundary*; the accent survives as an accent.
- **A settings choice is the session-view’s own radio** (Ed, 2026-08-18: *the non-slider choices in the cards are just radio buttons, and the session-view already has those*). Three controls became one: document-creation had `.opt`, founding-ceremony had `.rung`, and both were a hand-rolled radio in a bordered box standing a millimetre from `.lanepick` — which is the radio this system already has, and the one every member will have used a hundred times by the end of a session. A setting is exactly the judgment a lane bar puts: *one of these, or none of them yet*. The box went with it, and that is a gain: **open is said by depth, not by outline**, and a rule drawn round each option was competing with the radio for the job of saying which one is chosen.
- `--primary` — the one accent: every wire, every selection, every control that is on. `--ok` — green, meaning *decided* and only that.
- type scale `--t-lead / --t-body / --t-ui / --t-small / --t-cap / --t-micro` — **and every piece of text on the surface is on it** (housekeeping pass, 2026-08-17). It had six steps and the stylesheet used thirteen: 0.58, 0.62, 0.7, 0.75, 0.78, 0.8, 0.85 and 0.95rem all appeared as literals, most within half a pixel of a token that already existed. What is still allowed to be a literal is exactly one category, and naming it is what keeps the scale honest: a **glyph** size (`.achip` 1.125rem, the glyph buttons 1.2/1.35rem, the serif italic *I* 1.05rem) tunes a silhouette to a box rather than text to a scale, and has no business on the same ladder as prose. The `.eyebrow` class carries the single upper-case label treatment, at `--t-micro`, and it **names its users in its own selector** — `.headlab`, `.fieldlab`, `.rtag`, `.rechead`, `.pwhere` — because the same pass found the class had been written with the comment *one rule, so they cannot drift apart* and had zero consumers, while four hand-rolled copies of it sat at three different sizes. Each user keeps colour and layout and nothing else. A shared rule nothing points at is not a shared rule, it is one more opinion.
- **A commit row is one object, so its controls share a height** (housekeeping pass, 2026-08-17): 40px, set by the row, on every card. Width still belongs to the control — a glyph button is 52px, a labelled one as wide as its label. They are lifted on **every** card, not just the two where Ed first asked for it, since his reason (*they are actions for that box*) is as true of the ✓ and the ❄️ and the record's OK; and the two cards the old scoping missed hold the surface's only outline buttons, which are the ones with no fill to read as a control instead. Disabled controls stay flat. The **commit row is also the card's bottom band everywhere** — the three per-type notes and the lock note sit above it (Q310).
- spacing `--s1`–`--s5` on a 4px grid; radii `--r-sm / --r-md / --r-lg`; elevation `--shadow-sm/md/lg/xl`, neutral never tinted, and since Ed's QA of 2026-08-16 a **drop shadow rather than a glow** — each step is a 1px contact shadow plus one soft cast whose blur stays near 1.6× its offset, not Bootstrap's 3rem halo — **darker and less diffuse** since Ed, 2026-08-17, because once depth had to carry *open* on its own a wide faint cast became the halo the retune had removed; layout constants `--rail-left / --rail-right / --nav-h`.

**Product (UI and ceremony):**
- `setup.html` — **the one setup surface** (Ed, 361 + 362, 2026-08-18 evening): document-creation and founding-ceremony merged into `design/setup.html`, one live in-memory document from blank arrival to live motions, because the two files were the same screen seen by two people and were drifting exactly the way two copies of a design system drift. **Who you are is the dev dropdown**, top-left under the topbar (Ed, 362) — deliberately off the design system (dashed, monospace: a stagehand, not a member of the cast), listing the *actual* users: the founder, each person as they are invited, an applicant. Per-user state lives **on the roster rows** (answers, acknowledgments, motion drafts, consent answers, the one-🏛️-each); `S.myAns`/`S.okd`/`S.draft`/`S.mAns`/`S.myname…` are property accessors delegating to whoever is in the chair, so every existing predicate renders every seat unchanged. Beside the dropdown, **⏩ settle the founding** completes the founding in one press — two members invited, every answer filled *coherently with the founder defaults* so all the surface's sentences agree, gates open — because motions live on the far side of the founding and a mock with no persistence would otherwise cost ten minutes of clicking per QA run. **Motions gate on `canJudge()`** (§9.6a: before the start nothing is amended, only set — the founder re-sets freely, so the composer appears only once the constitution is settled). **What stands on a delegated setting is derived live from the room's answers** (`takenOf`/`standsOf`: the consent rule computed — highest for quorum and the bar, latest for the ending, longest for lapse, most private for the privacy family, most restrictive for machines, most generous for the rate), never the founder's field, which still says who holds it; the composer heads, the consent “replaces X” line and the lane filtering all speak it. **The composers are session-view's own** (Ed, 2026-08-18: *the composers on the session-view are the better ones*): lanes are cards.js `laneBarHtml` speaking `data-mval`, the rationale is the editing-card's sealed-disc speaker with `data-placeholder` — never an avatar, since authorship is sealed — and the PROPOSE map covers **every** composable setting (title, link, pacing, rate, ending, bar, quorum, lapse, the three privacy ladders, visibility, machines, applications, the membership) so nothing falls through to a bare text box. Post-start the founder keeps direct edit on **reserved** settings (the 👑 rule, consented on the way in) and composes like any member on delegated ones; a member's motion on a reserved setting carries the 👑-question note. The assembly-press convenes the *live arrived members* around the 🏛️. Ordinary and constitutional motion cards, the withdraw-returns-whole rule and per-viewer judged tallies all render from one `cardFor`. **Since the swap of 2026-08-18 (night) the page renders over `@draft/constitution`** (plan 367a, four probe-gated slices): a `ConstitutionSession` is born at the save (§9.7a) and holds everything the ROOM holds — settled values, delegations, blind counts, resolutions (`takenOf` now reads the module's consent resolution, not a page reimplementation), gates, motions, the register — while the page keeps rendering, its copy, and the **provisional layer** (radio positions, unsent answers, a motion being typed; the S fields survive as exactly that and nothing more). Motions **resolve and apply** now: typed values ride the composer as `d.val`, the settle check runs in the fold, a carried amendment changes the sentence at the head of the document, and a reserved setting's carried change renders the founder a 👑 Accept/Reject card. Ordinary adjudication is a **dev seam** (all-judged majority); the real adjudicator exists since 367b (`engine-bridge`), and wiring it into the page is Q391's design work — an ordinary motion should be served as a pairwise race card, not an everyone-votes tally. The contamination guard is `design/tools/setup-probe.js` against `design/reference/setup-pre-constitution/` (tag `pre-constitution`): 34 steps, two allowlisted diffs (the p-fixture progress tallies became real counts), everything else byte-identical. **Live since Q391** (2026-08-19): served from the product server the page detects its address — `/` is the birth, `/d/:slug` a real document rendered through `remoteCS`, a ConstitutionSession-lookalike over the blind `view()` (reads from the projection, commands as serialized POSTs folded under the cookie's identity, values re-hydrated on a 4s poll but never under the open card, dev chrome hidden, the ordinary judgment a real `judge-race` on the served card). Opened as a file or from the static design server it is the fixture it always was — one page, three addresses.
- `document-creation` — **merged into `setup.html`** (Ed 361, 2026-08-18 evening; the filename redirects). Kept for the reasoning, which all survives in the merged file: the screen where every §9.0 setting is chosen when a document is made, and the roster is invited. Ed called it the "draft creation screen"; **`draft` already means a candidate patch** everywhere else in this project, so the surface is `document-creation` and the thing it makes is a document or a charter. Ed wanted to see every option before any are cut (Q259). **Arrival makes the order of existence the order of the cards** (Ed, 2026-08-18): going to docs.vote presents a brand-new *unsaved* document whose rail holds one task, 🪶 Title — the title is the first question, **and opening it is your click** (Ed, 2026-08-20, reversing a briefly-built auto-open: *the card should not open on arrival; we want to teach users that they have to click on tasks / tabs* — nothing on this surface opens itself, arrival included). Answering it spawns the 🔗 card pre-filled with a slug generated from the title, checked unique (a collision takes a short suffix). **At the live birth the verification mail is real and the pretend inbox never opens over it** (same QA): the mail-modal is fixture furniture, and the 📧 card's sent state says where the mail actually is — your inbox, whose link is what saves the document. **Confirming the link is what saves the document**; until then nothing exists anywhere, and nobody can be invited, because an invitation *is* the link (SPEC §9.7a). The opening order is Ed’s, card by card: **title → 📧 your email → link → 🎩 am I a member → the rest** (membership, name, picture, starting text), each born as the one before settles. The email is **verified before anything is saved** (Ed, 2026-08-18): the mail — *you have created a document called [title], log in to create it* — is itself the login, so clicking it proves the address and carries you to the save; it comes before the link because this address is the only way back in, and while nothing exists a typo costs nothing. The mockup renders the mail as a preview whose button is the magic link — your inbox is right there on the card. Sybil wariness at this front door is parked as Q346. **It is `session-view` with nothing in it yet** (Ed, 2026-08-17, replacing a first pass that was a form): same three columns, same rails, same card-over-document gesture. The convenor pastes the starting text into the column it will later be edited in, the `contents-rail` assembles itself as they go, the title is a placeholder in the topbar you click, and the right-hand rail holds one card per question that must be settled before anybody can draft — each opening a modal over the document. The reason it works is that the settings are not a form: they are **a queue of things that want you**, which is exactly what that rail already means. **And they open in the document rather than over it** (Ed, 2026-08-18) — see `setup-band`; the modal was the last object on this screen that was not the session-view. Markdown hashes do not survive the paste, because this is the column the document will be edited in and session-view has no markdown in it; and a leading `# Title` becomes the title and leaves the document, since a charter usually begins with its own name and there is a field for that in the bar. Live constraints: perpetual forces a fixed bar, and the drip changes from *per % of the window* to *per hour* with it. **A card with a dependency does not appear until its dependency is settled** (Ed, 2026-08-18) rather than appearing greyed: anything the room can be asked waits on the **roster**, since *the participants decide* means nothing until there are participants, and the constitution waits on the **window**, since a rising bar needs an endpoint. So the rail opens at five cards and grows to fourteen. **“Who may be seen” is three cards** (Ed, 2026-08-18) — 👤 whose proposal it is, ✍️ signing, 👁️ your judgments — because they are three rules decided and resolved separately, and one card holding three ladders is a form pretending to be a question. Their glyphs are three silhouettes rather than three shades of one idea, since all three are about being seen. **The link is its own card too** (🔗, Ed, 2026-08-18): a slug had been a read-only field in the share box, which made it the one thing on the screen decided by not being decided.  
  - `setup-queue` — that right-hand rail. **Setup tasks speak the session-view's own lifecycle** (Ed, 2026-08-18: *they are yellow when open, ⏳ while waiting for other input, grey when decided, they lose their custom emoji and just become ✔s*): five states in `setup.js` (`stateOf`/`markOf`) — **ask** (yellow, wearing the subject glyph, the one state where the glyph is the mark, since a setup rail is many questions in one state and the informative mark is *which*), **wait** (⏳ grey, in the rail with its fill as the watching UI; gates wait as pile tabs only), **news** (drawn ✔ on the changed-green wash, pinned until OK — the unacknowledged-decision rule reaching resolved blind questions, rules that bind you, and gates opening), **yours** (✏️ blue, a submitted application, fill = judging progress), **done** (rail entry: drawn grey ✔, and it leaves; pile tab: **keeps its subject glyph** on the grey wash — Ed, same day: the piles stand in one place, most of their contents can still be acted on, *it's basically a menu* — with the constitution block's lines as buttons besides). Full walk-through and the founding **dependency tree** (edges marked literal vs pacing, Ed's distinction) in `design/setup.notes.md` (deleted 2026-08-23; `git show aba22fd:design/setup.notes.md`); residual calls in Q350. **Every constitutional setting defaults to *The participants decide*** (Ed, 2026-08-17), modelled until 2026-08-19 as a **third value of the setting** — superseded by `governance-tabs` (Q403): delegation is now the state of holding neither power, said by the 🔧/👑 tabs, and no value list carries it. What is left for the convenor is the text, the title, the window and the roster; everything else is the room’s unless taken back. A delegated card is **settled, not outstanding** — answering by deciding not to answer — so it goes grey like any other finished one. **The whole constitution is dependency-paced** (Ed, 2026-08-19, answering 409 as (a): *pacing of constitutional tasks is very fast … make sure they are not shown before they are needed*) — the rule that already carried title → 📧 → 🔗 now carries everything after the save, in **waves, one per section of the constitution, in the order it reads**: 🪪, then 💤 🚪, then ⏱️ 🤖, then ⏰ 👥, then ✒️ 📈, then 👤 ✍️ 👁️, with 🌍 riding the document's own run behind 📄. Measured before: confirming the register alone bore nine tasks in a single frame. And **a delegated question waits for a member who has arrived** (Ed, closing 408) — an answer-task exists for nobody while the founder is alone, since at an electorate of one the founder's own answer resolves the blind question solo, delegation self-defeating in one click — **unless there is nothing else outstanding**, Ed's own amendment, because a rail that has emptied itself while the room fills up is worse than an early answer (`ansDue`). **And the answer tasks cascade too** (Ed, closing 416: *if I have to do all of them anyway, they should cascade*) — they ride the settings' own wave graph, looking **through** any dependency nobody is asked about, since most of the graph is settings the founder holds and a wave of those would otherwise let every question behind it past at once. Measured: three, two, one, three, against eight in one frame. The module closes the other end (Q413, SPEC §9.0b v0.61): **a blind question does not resolve while an invitation is outstanding, and never on one voice at all** — a consent rule computed over a single answer *is* that answer, so a founder standing alone may answer and nothing settles until somebody is actually there; §9.6a's one-unopened-email worry is answered by §9.6a's own remedy, the founder's free hand on the roster until judging opens. **Nothing is owed an OK until the document begins** (Q414): §9.6a lets the founder re-set freely until then, so an OK collected before the start acknowledges a rule that may not be the rule — a gate opening stays the exception, being a thing that happened rather than a rule that was set. **The watcher waits for your own answer** (Ed, same day): a delegated setting stood in the rail twice, the ⏳ counting the room and your own question, which is the same title twice and the wrong one first. And **a task you have to do carries no subtitle** (Ed, on 📧: *the queue-card does not need "Not given yet", that's why it's yellow*) — subtitles help you choose which task to open, so they survive only on a **motion** (the value proposed) and on **news** (what happened). 📈 pacing left the delegable settings entirely (Q415, v0.61): it is the founder's at creation and hands over like the title rather than opening a founding question nobody could answer.
  - `birth-pass` — **what is born arrives, it does not appear** (Ed, 2026-08-19, and its transition chosen as 410(a)+(d)). Both rails and the whole band are rebuilt wholesale on every render, so nothing on screen has an identity across one — which is why every entrance on this surface is the wash-fade trick applied to something else: `birthPass` (setup.js) keys each element by what it is *about* (`data-para`, section id, `data-q`), remembers every key ever seen, and hands a newborn its full presence after one forced reflow. Two entrances by what the element does to its neighbours: a **rail entry grows from zero height while it fades** (the jarring half of a rail birth is the neighbours jumping down in one frame — growing makes them part), a **document paragraph or section only fades** (text that slides while you read it is worse than text that arrives). A batch **cascades in document order**, 55ms apart, capped at six, each column counting its own — several tasks born at once are several things that happened, and one frame says they are one thing. Reduced motion fades everything and moves nothing; a stagehand act (seat switch, ⏩) mutes the pass rather than replaying fifteen entrances at once.
  - `card-morph` — **switching cards within one strip is a morph, not a teardown** (Ed, 2026-08-19: *transition between tabs in one stack is jerky*). The box survives: its height glides from the old card's to the new card's while the body crossfades, and the head — which is where the strip lives — swaps in place, so the tab you clicked never blinks and never moves. Measured on the 👥 stack: card top and tab top both move **0.0px** while the height runs 648 → 247. From anywhere else the old card still closes into its own paragraph first and the new one opens out of its, because there the document *would* jump by a card's height in one frame. `clip-path` rather than `overflow: hidden`, for the same reason the open/close animation needed it — the strip hangs outside the card's box.
  - `commit-row grammar` — **discard, commit, and OK** (Ed, 2026-08-19, closing 412). **🗑️** *put it back as it stands* is the left of every commit row you can commit, disabled when there is nothing to put back; since a radio writes straight into `S`, what-it-stands-as is **remembered rather than derived** — one snapshot per opening, taken after the module has had its say. The **commit** is ✒️ where the act is a set of your own (the 🔧/👑 power tabs included — giving up a power is a unilateral act), ✓ where it is an answer or a judgment, **✏️ Propose** / **🏛️ Hold to ask everyone** where it is a motion, one glyph per route. **OK** is a third thing and not a quieter commit: what a card wears when it asks nothing of you but to have seen it (Ed's words) — a gate opening, a decision you are owed. "Close" survives only where the card holds nothing to put back: 🪪, whose invitations commit as you send them, and 📄, whose value lives in the document column. **An emoji needs a quiet ground** (Ed, same day: *icon button backgrounds shouldn't be very saturated*) — a saturated fill fights a coloured glyph for the same pixels, so ✒️ sits on the accent-subtle ground, which also splits the commits by hue: the pen wears the colour this surface uses for your own work, and the drawn ✓ stays the one solid green thing on a card.
  - `setup-band` — **the constitution, at the top of the document** (Ed, 2026-08-18: *instead of modals they open cards in the document at the top, above the text, pushing it down like decision-cards do a clause; they have cables and tabs*). A modal was the wrong object twice over: it lifted the question off the document at exactly the moment the question is *about* the document, and it made these the only cards on the surface that do not behave like cards. A decision card replaces the clause it is about; a setting is about the whole document, so it opens where the whole document begins. It draws a `queue-wire` to its rail entry and carries the same lifecycle wash, and a settled setting leaves a grey tab — so the screen ends in **a tab group at the head of the document, next to the title**, one grey tab per rule the room agreed, which is where you go to ask what the bar is.
  - `gate-cards` — 💡 **Proposing** (✏️ until Ed's glyph pass of 2026-08-19 — ✏️ means an ordinary proposal in the preamble's own sentence, so the gate wearing it said the wrong thing) and ⚖️ **Judging**, the two things a member cannot do yet, as cards (Ed, 2026-08-18, 330b). They were a topbar status line and a three-step strip at the top of the rail, which was the last chrome on a surface whose whole argument is that everything wanting something is a card. A gate is not a setting — nobody decided it, it follows from the others — but it is exactly the shape of an unacknowledged decision, so it takes that rule: **a ⏳ tab in the pile while it waits**, out of the rail, since nothing is being asked of you; **news-green with a drawn ✔ and an OK** the moment it opens (the grammar pass of 2026-08-18 — an unacknowledged decision is green news, not a hot ask); gone from the rail once you press it, and a grey ✔ tab in the pile after. It carries no kind line and its head says *not a decision · it follows from the others*. They stand in the **members'** pile: the pile is everything the members hold, and being able to write is one of those. What each waits on is drawn as the cards themselves, so it reads as *these, and then you can write* rather than as a rule.
- `motion-controls` — **a motion is composed with the setting's own control** (Ed, 2026-08-18: *surely you should be presented with the roster… but instead I have a blank text box?* and *surely it should give me radio: a date picker / never*). `motionCompose` had written a free-text lane for every setting, inherited from the `editing-card`, where the thing proposed genuinely **is** prose. A setting is not prose: the surface already knows how to collect a date, a membership, a number and a choice, and it collected this very value once already — so a text box asks somebody to guess a format that then has to be parsed, and on the membership it asks them to type a name when what they want is the fourteen people in front of them with an ✕ beside each. The lane is now a **slot** the surface fills with that setting's own control, and only falls back to prose where the value is prose. The rationale stays a lane, because a rationale is prose everywhere. **One step since the Applications example** (Ed, 2026-08-18): the settled card IS the composer — the current rule at the top as the clause, the other options below as the setting's own radios (what stands marked inert), a rationale lane, and 🗑️ with the route's commit (the 🏛️ hold, or Propose) at the bottom; picking an option is what starts the motion, and the 201c✏️ Propose a change201d step is retired. **Exhaustive since Ed's QA of 2026-08-18** (*ditto Link card, Membership card, etc*): every composable setting has its control — title a single-line input, the link its slug beside the fixed docs.vote/ prefix (with the no-link-ever-breaks note), pacing its rising/fixed pair, the drip its number, the policy its own rungs with what stands marked inert — and 📄 Starting Text **composes nowhere**, because the text is changed by proposing in the document itself and a motion button there was a second door to the same room.
- `setup-piles` — **superseded 2026-08-18 (evening)**: both piles dissolved into the `constitution-section`’s subtitled sections — every decision is a paragraph with its tab, every member task stands in the section it decides, and the `tab-stack` machinery survives only in the live document’s gutter. Kept for the history of the reasoning: **two `tab-stack`s at the head of the document, one per holder** (Ed, 2026-08-18: *I'd like all of these tabs to act like clause-tabs and pile like clause tabs. The convenor's settings sit in one pile, and participants' in the pile below*), retiring the flat tab group of the same morning and answering Q316 the other way. The flat row was defended on the grounds that a pile of fourteen shows one glyph and a constitution deserves better; that argument was really about the wrong things being in one pile. **Two piles say who decides**, which a flat row of fourteen could not say at all, and they say it in the vocabulary the gutter already has — the front tab is what most wants you, the slivers carry hue and no count, one stack is one target, and opening it expands the pile into the strip down the card's left edge. Each pile stands in its own `setrow`, which is the card's own box, so the pile occupies exactly the gutter column its strip will: the tab you click does not move. `fitBand` floors the row against the pile beside it, since there is no clause here to give a row a height.
    One behaviour is now visible that was previously only in the rail: **handing a setting to the room makes a question card appear in the membership pile** (Ed, 2026-08-18, revising the same morning’s tab-moves-piles design and closing Q323 with it — nothing jumps, so nothing can jump *while open*). The setting card stays in the convenor’s pile with the delegation radio and the watching half; the question card that is born below is **the same card a member gets**, and it is where the convenor — a member like anybody else once 🎩 says so — answers later, in their own time (*you can set “delegate to membership” at start and then answer your own question later*). Same glyph, same title, told apart by whose pile it stands in; taken back, the question card vanishes. Delegation stops being a radio you ticked and becomes a thing you watch happen — a birth rather than a teleport. And one departure from the gutter, which is a difference in the objects rather than in the design: **a settled setting stays in the closed pile**, where a filed decision leaves. A filed decision is history at that clause; a settled setting is not history, it is *the rule*. It goes grey and stays, which is what leaves the head of the document holding the constitution. Q319 asks where the piles live once drafting starts. **Half of that sentence was reversed on 2026-08-27** — the pile now also holds one grey record chip per settled motion, behind the rule: see *A settled motion files behind its rule (2026-08-27, Q942)* at the foot of this file.
  - `reserved` / 👑 — **the two powers are ✒️ (drafting directly) and 🛡️ (assent) since Ed, 2026-08-20** — *a philosophical sword and shield*: one writes, one only refuses, which is what 🔧/👑 could never say, since a wrench and a crown are not opposites. 👑 survives as the **mark beside a person** who holds either power anywhere, 📯 the mark of one who holds none; the two half-states now have characters — **pen without shield**, a founder who *does* things (the membership: they invite alone, carried applications need nobody), and **shield without pen**, a pure veto, the constitutional monarch (the threshold). What follows describes the build under the retired glyphs. **a reservation is two powers since v0.54** (Ed, 2026-08-19, with a same-day correction): **unilateral change** (the founder changes the setting directly) and **assent** (a member-carried change waits on the founder's accept — the 👑 question exists exactly where this power is held). Held and relinquished separately, one-way, the reserve motion restoring either or both (Q394); Ed's own examples are the membership held unilateral-only (founder invites alone, carried applications need nobody's accept) and the bar held assent-only (members present changes for assent, the founder cannot re-set it to taste). **Timing, per the correction**: delegation keeps its earlier clock, giving up assent is available from creation, giving up unilateral change waits until proposing opens (the assent-only state is inert pre-start). The register's pair lives inside the applications value (`reserved` / `reserved-unilateral` / `reserved-assent` / `members`). 👑 = either power on anything; 📯 = none anywhere. Built in `@draft/constitution` (`SettingState.powers`, `relinquish`), and **on the surface since Q396 closed as 396c** (Ed, 2026-08-19: *make it easy for the founder to decide, and then write the constitution in precise language*): at setup the value list keeps *The members decide* and a kept value wakes the **“And afterwards” pair** (`powerTail`/`registerTail` in setup.html) — assent kept / members change it without you — with the third state (stop changing it directly) offered only once the document is live, one-way from there; the constitution paragraph states whichever state holds in one precise third-person sentence (`holderLine`, `regLine`), so the four alternatives stand side by side where rules are read. Pre-start the pair is as revisable as any radio (§9.6a — the module's `reclaim` restores a relinquished power on a held setting without touching its value); the founder's direct hand on a card IS the unilateral power (`founderDirect`), so an assent-only setting renders the founder the member composer; and the composer's carried-to-👑 note keys on the assent power (`crownWaits`), so a unilateral-only setting truthfully promises no crown question. The pre-v0.54 story follows, still true wherever both powers travel together: **reserved** is the word for not-delegated (Ed, 2026-08-18): every setting is either the members’ (delegated) or **reserved by the convenor**, and the band labels say which. Every convenor is a **📯** or a **👑** (Ed, 2026-08-18; 🏴→📯 same day): 📯 if the membership is the members’, 👑 if they reserved it — the title names the person, not the document, and the mark stands beside their name in the Constitution block. The 👑: they keep unilateral invite-and-remove past the start — consented once, by everybody, on the way in, since the 👑 stands beside their name in the Constitution block and nobody joins a crowned convenor’s document unknowingly. The crown **lapses like a member** (§9.5a’s clock runs on the convenor too), and **lapse is automatic abstention** (Ed, 2026-08-18, v0.49, superseding the holder transfer): assent on reserved matters grants itself while the crown sleeps, nothing changes hands, and revival — just logging in — restores the requirement. What keeps a monarchy mortal is that an absent king can refuse nothing. **Reserved is assent, not silence** (Ed, 2026-08-18): anybody may motion a reserved setting — by its own kind's route, which reservation never alters (v0.49) — and a change the membership passes goes to the convenor as a **👑 question**, theirs to accept or reject, **at the end of either route** (Ed, same day: *not just membership, any reserved decision* — the constitutional-route exemption died with it, since consent's everyone does not include a clerk). **The name-mark follows any reservation** (Ed, 2026-08-18, closing Q379 wide): 👑 = reserves *anything*, 📯 = reserves *nothing*, and **a founder who reserves nothing and is not a member has no role at all** — every post-start convenor power flows through reservation, so at zero they are a name in the record (SPEC §9.7 v0.50). The starting text never crowns (no post-start change route — the text is changed by drafting). Zero is reachable since v0.51, and since v0.52 (Ed, 2026-08-19) **the verb is delegate and it is never a button**: "The title is delegated to the members" is a constitutional sentence among the setting's own options, preferred and ✒️'d — the founder may **delegate anything they hold from the moment proposing opens** (title and link at text-confirm; a delegable setting still opens its founding question until judging starts, and hands over its settled value after; the membership through the applications card, both holds at once, its crown living in that value). One-way; **the road back is a constitutional `reserve` motion** on any such setting (the membership's via its own value) that lands **without the founder's assent**, since the release from an unwanted crown is delegation itself — which is also why the room can crown a **lapsed** founder (Ed: *a constitutional monarchy* — powers held in name, auto-abstained on everything until they log back in). The zero-reservation founder keeps one standing property: the membership can return reservations to them. Out of scope by Ed's word (2026-08-19): reserving the text (Q387) and any individual delegation / foundership transfer / inheritance (Q388 — *a benevolent dictator for life*, nobody inherits a lapsed crown). The 👑 question is **a task of its own kind** (news-pinned, Accept / Reject its commit row); one pending at crown lapse **passes automatically**; and the crown’s control over reserved matters is **unilateral** — direct change, no motion — while anybody else’s proposal requires assent (Ed, 2026-08-18, closing Q351; SPEC v0.45). **The kind eyebrow left the card heads** with it — “🏛️ Constitutional · reserved by the convenor” said in chrome what the controls convey: a constitutional compose commits with the 🏛️ hold, a reserved one ends at the 👑 question. ~~Only the membership crowns its convenor~~ (superseded by Q379-wide, above: any reservation crowns). Residuals in Q349. SPEC §9.7, v0.40.
- `governance-tabs` — **🔧 became ✒️ and 👑 became 🛡️ on 2026-08-20** (Ed, Q454 — see `wallets`; what follows describes the build as it stands, under the retired glyphs). **Every held-able setting is a tab group** (Ed, 2026-08-19, closing 403/404/405; SPEC §9.7 v0.59): the value's own tab in front, and beneath it two power tabs — 🔧 *May the Founder Change This Directly?* (unilateral change; Ed's glyph and his reading: the founder gets things going, then steps back — chosen over ⚙️, which would be the one glyph meaning “settings” on a surface where everything is one) and 👑 *Do Carried Changes Need the Founder's OK?* (assent). Drawn with the existing pile machinery (`ctx.chipsFor` names the pile in setup.html; setup.js's `para` renders it and opens any member of the group at the setting's own paragraph with the whole group as the strip); a power card's head is that power's rule as it stands (`powerHeadLine`), its body the give-up radios (`powerBody`), its commit `commitPower` — revisable pre-start (§9.6a, reclaim-then-redrop), one-way after. **Delegation is the state of holding neither power** (Q403 — the module already derived holder from powers; its one new seam: pre-start, giving up the second power on a delegable setting emits `setting-delegated`, opening the blind founding question): “The members decide” left every value list, `theyDecide` renders a members-hold note instead, and picking a value on a members-held setting is the taking-back. This retires the 2026-08-17 “third value of the setting” modelling of delegation, the 🗝️ title/link governance cards of the same morning (Q402 superseded — the title's clause is now a lead paragraph, *The document is titled “X”.*, wearing just the two power tabs since its value is the heading), and the “And afterwards” pair in card bodies (`powerTail`/`holdChoice`/`mayBy`/`govBody` deleted). **The constitution states governance by deviation under a preamble** (Q404): the opening paragraph states the defaults once (anyone may propose; ✏️ at the threshold, 🏛️ by consent; nobody direct, nothing waits on assent, unless a rule says so), and `holderLine` appends only the deviations — a delegated clause says nothing. Ed's second elision default (proposals closed unless granted) is deliberately vacuous — reserved is assent, not silence. One truth-rule the build forced: a members-held setting whose value stands (a post-start hand-over) states its value like any settled rule — the “n of E have answered” line keys on the module's `collecting`, never on fixture progress. Not yet in the pattern: 🤝 Applications (its pair rides the value — `registerTail` survives) and the 📄 text (never held).
- `task` — the general term for *a thing that gets tracked by sidebar cards and opens a document card with the controls*; a **card** is the UX element that represents it (Ed, 2026-08-18, closing Q347). Copy says tasks; the design system keeps naming the objects cards; “queue-card” never appears in anything a member reads.
- `applications-card` — 🤝 **Applications** (Ed's rename, 2026-08-18, from *Membership Policy*; glyph 🤝 — free since 🏛️ took the constitutional route), split from 🪪 **Membership** (Ed, 2026-08-18: *separate "Membership" which is the register of people who are members from "Membership Policy" which is how people become members after the ceremony*): the register card keeps who is in and the inviting; the policy card holds **who holds the membership** (reserved → 👑, or the members’) and the **join policy**. Constitutional, on both surfaces; its band label follows its own holder radio. The split also un-swallowed a real bug — the old single card carried two readNote keys, and the reserved note was silently overridden. SPEC §9.7½, v0.42.
- `applicants` — a subsection of Membership (Ed, 2026-08-18): the people asking to be in the room. Hangs off the 🤝 Applications card’s **join policy**, four rungs: *invitation only* · *applications must be proposed* (a member proposes it, staking the ✏️ — **a second is a proposed application**, Ed, closing Q348a) · *anyone may apply* (straight to the bar, free — the tasks its price, the bar its filter) · *open*. Where application is allowed, a non-member reader’s rail holds one task — Apply for membership — which spawns four more: 📧 **an email, verified by magic link before anything can be submitted** — the applicant’s **identity**, and member emails are **unique** (one address, one member; an address already on the membership is told to log in instead, which also kills Q346’s double-invitation vector), then ✋ name, 🖼️ picture, 📝 a few optional words; an empty application is a real application; nothing collected before Begin, nothing sent before Submit. A submitted application becomes an **ordinary motion to grant membership** — deliberately a different door from invitation-by-consent, squared by the holder reading (the policy is the constitutional act, each application its ordinary consequence). **How it is judged closed as a race** (Ed, 2026-08-19, Q397, SPEC §9.7½ v0.56): each admit motion is its **own one-candidate race against the membership as it stands** — a synthetic per-applicant engine setting (`admit:<id>`), which is what makes two applicants *structurally* unable to be raced against each other (Ed's words: judged separately). The author is the seconder under *proposed* (✏️ staked, rationale required) or the applicant themself under *apply* (their words the rationale; a voice in the engine for exactly that act, suspended out of E in the same breath). **Every proposal to bring somebody in offers a rationale** (Ed, 2026-08-19, in two rulings — the second relaxing the first, v0.57): the lane is always in the composer and the case rides the motion for the members to read, but a blank rationale is a real proposal — the module never refuses one, exactly as an empty application is a real application. Live, members meet applicants in the **Applicants block** on the open 🪪 card (name, words, Admit / Keep the membership as it is / Indifferent + ✓ riding `judge-race`); the applicant's own four-card seat is still fixture. The applicant’s whole view is the ceremony surface’s fourth topbar state (**An applicant**), and the Applicants line under the Membership heading shows each with how far their judging has got. SPEC §9.7½.
- `lapse` — 💤 *Do memberships lapse?*, the constitutional setting that closed Q345 (Ed, 2026-08-18): whether an **inactive** membership lapses ("quiet" became "inactive" on the surface, Ed, same day) and after how long, each member stating the **shortest quiet spell** they would accept and the document taking the longest, *never* the longest of all. A lapsed member leaves the quorum base like an abstainer, keeps their cast judgments counted, is warned by email first, receives **the package** (the document as it stands + the record to date) at the lapse, and revives by simply logging in — consented rule, no motion. SPEC §9.5a. Nineteen cards.
- `mail-modal` — the verification email renders as a **modal standing for your inbox**, deliberately OFF the design system — no tokens, Arial, an email client’s idea of a page — because it previews another medium and will soon be a real email whose look must owe nothing to this surface (Ed, 2026-08-18: *put the mockup email into a modal … we don’t want it to pollute our design system*). Its copy lives in `MAILS`, a template object one substitution away from the real transactional template.
- `alpha-flag` — **“Warning: docs.vote is in alpha!”**, fixed bottom-left of every page (Ed, 2026-08-20, answering 481 as (a): the domain points at the alpha service, so the room deserves to know what it is drafting in). Quiet by the palette’s own rule — it asks nothing of you, so it takes no hot colour and no glyph; a permanent banner that shouted would fight every card on the surface for a whole session. At `--t-small` rather than `--t-micro`, because a warning nobody can read is decoration, and `pointer-events: none`, because it stands over the document’s own margin and must never eat a click meant for the text. The `devswitch` sits above it (bottom 47px), so the stagehand and the flag never overlap; live mode hides the stagehand and the flag stands alone. Deliberately **not** on the magic-link interstitial, which submits itself on arrival — a flag there would only flash.
- `constitution-section` — the head of the document’s first heading is **Constitution**, not “Convenor” (Ed, 2026-08-18), and since the same evening the constitution is **document text with sections** (two passes, hours apart). Under 🎩 who convened it and ⏱️ when it was *constituted*, **five subtitled sections, each with a line of prose saying what it governs** (Ed: *these sections should each have proper subtitles, with the appropriate text underneath*): **Membership** (two subsections since Ed's same-evening pass: *Members* — the list itself, 🪪 the invitations tab beside it, your own row at the bottom wearing the **You stack**: ✋ 🖼️ and, since Ed 2026-08-19, 📧 — everyone logged in has an address they may want to change or re-verify, per-seat state, an arrived member's verified by construction since the invitation link WAS the login — and *Applications*, the 🤝 decision with the *Applicants* list under it; then lapse, **🚪 removal** (Q401a: *How Is a Member Removed?* — everyone's consent theirs included / everyone but them, the 🏛️-without class / an ordinary race; the subject always sees it, is never asked under 'others', and the ❌ door composes by whatever rung stands), machine member and the gates), **Decisions** (the ending, quorum, the close threshold, pacing), **Anonymity** (Ed's rename, 2026-08-19, from *Privacy*: proposer naming, signing, judgment reveal), **Proposals** (the preamble — moved here from the head of the constitution, Ed 2026-08-19, in his wording, keeping the nobody-direct/no-assent baseline sentence — then the gates, rate, machines), **The Document** (title, link, starting text). **The section headings carry no intro prose** (Ed, 2026-08-19: the sections are not big enough to need explanations, and the clauses ought to be self-explanatory). Within a section, **one distinct paragraph per decision, its tab in the gutter to its left** — clause-tab geometry — and the open card **replaces its own paragraph**, taking its tab with it, so the tab you click does not move. **A question the members answer stands as a task in the section it decides** (Ed: *choices to be made BY THE MEMBERS about the constitution … tasks for members associated with the relevant constitutional section*): on the ceremony the rule and the question are one paragraph wearing one tab; on the convenor surface a delegated setting’s paragraph (watching, ⏳) is followed by a *Your answer* task paragraph via `ctx.tasksFor`. The rails group by the same section titles. **The document reads identically to every reader — only their tasks differ** (Ed, 2026-08-18): the constitution2019s sentences are third person (201cThe Convenor has delegated to members to decide2026201d), never 201cyou201d; what varies by reader is which tabs stand in their gutter and which cards open — with one sanctioned exception, 201cyou201d: in the members list you are always at the bottom, on your own line, wearing your ✋🖼️ tabs (Ed, same day). This retires the compact `constitution-block` **and both `setup-piles`** — the section names and intro prose are drafts of mine awaiting Ed’s edit. **A settled card's head is the rule, not the task's name** (Ed's QA, 2026-08-19: *many constitutional decision cards still have their title at the top instead of the constitutional text*): `headFor` serves the paragraph's own sentence — `decisionLine`, gates included — as the clause at the head of the open card, the title surviving only on open questions (a question is its title), on the membership (the list is the clause) and on 📄 Starting Text, whose settled card instead shows **the text the document started from** (`cs.text`, immutable once confirmed). **The document begins with its own name** (Ed, same QA): under a hairline closing the constitutional parts, the title stands again as the charter's heading with the 📄 tab beside it (left-aligned on the prose's own edge — Ed's QA, 2026-08-19: the topbar's `.doctitle` padding was shifting its text 24px right), and joins the contents rail between the constitution's sections and the text's own headings. **The constitution opens with the title and link clauses** (Ed, 2026-08-19, thrice: first as two 🗝️ governance cards — *the title card is doing two jobs* — then generalized into the `governance-tabs` pattern that dissolved them; then the preamble left for the Proposals section and the title clause gained its value tab): *The document is titled “X”.* / *The document lives at docs.vote/x.* stand first with their deviations appended by `holderLine`, the title clause wearing the full governance tab group — **🪶 the value tab in front, 🔧/👑 beneath** (Ed: *move title tab to the first clause once that clause appears*), so post-save the title card opens at its own clause with the clause sentence as its head, while at the birth the tab still stands beside the big heading and the card still opens onto it (`fitTitleCard` runs only pre-save). **The top title sits on the document's own left edge** (Ed's QA, same day: it was indented — `.doc .doctitle`'s side padding and auto-centring were the indent). **🌍 Visibility stands as the penultimate clause of that opening run** (Ed, same day: privacy is about members, not the document), before the Founded line and out of the Privacy section, its rail entry riding The Document group. **The Founded line carries the founding moment** — *Founded by [avatar] [name] 👑 at [time] on [date]* — stamped from `constitutedAtT` (the start; §9.6a — never known before it), and the fixture's logical clock is based at load time so a stamp renders as today. **Headings fold** (Ed, same day): session-view's own `sectoggle` ▸, inline in the constitution's section headings and on an overlay rail beside the prose's headings (a button inside contenteditable would become harvested text), mirrored in the TOC; a folded section keeps its heading, and a section never folds while it holds the open card. **And clicking a heading's text folds it** (Ed, 2026-08-19): a plain click on any heading — prose headings, the constitution's sections, and the Constitution heading itself, which folds the whole constitution leaving the charter below — toggles the fold; a drag-selection or shift-click is editing and survives, which is what keeps prose headings editable (highlight, then type; double-click still selects a word — its two clicks fold and unfold, net nothing). The lvl3 subsection headings don't fold yet (Q406). **The constitution's links are real links** (Ed, same day): `linkify` wraps docs.vote addresses in `.doclink` anchors — ink-coloured, underlined, the underline waking on hover — applied after escaping, in the decision paragraphs and card heads. **Inviting and removing are their own cards** (Ed, closing 400 as (a) with the glyphs): ✉️ *Invite a Member* and ❌ *Remove a Member*, doors rather than decisions — always settled, standing in the 🪪 stack from the moment judging opens, composing the membership motions that used to share one roster composer; a running invite/remove motion renders on its own door (`motionTargets` routes by payload kind), the 🪪 card keeps the register (and the pre-start invitation box, which post-start survives only where the founder's direct power is real), and removal offers no founder-direct path pending Q401's removal-policy setting. **Title's glyph is 🪶** (Ed, 2026-08-19, retiring the same morning's drawn home plate; the link's is 📍, retiring 🔗 — both changed in the module catalogue too, and the reserved-faces scan keeps the retired glyphs reserved). And **a blank or never-entered name renders as “Anonymous”** (Ed, same day, SPEC §9.0c) — a name, not a gap; an invitee who has not arrived is still listed by address, since the email is how invitees are listed before a name exists. A paragraph states the **document’s** rule, never your own answer, blindness intact; the 🪪 membership card keeps **the membership list as its clause** via `ctx.clauseFor`.
- `answer-bodies` — the consent controls (`slider`, `ladder`) and the per-question copy (`ANSWER`), in setup.js since Q344 closed (Ed, 2026-08-18): a drafter-convenor answers the questions they delegated **on their own surface**, as **a separate question card in the membership pile** (Ed, same day, revising a first build that grew the answering body inside the setting card) — 🎩 decides whether answers are owed at all, the question card is born when the setting is delegated, it stays outstanding, and the surface’s Proposing gate waits, until answered (§9.0b).
