mail rides the fold (Resend via `RESEND_API_KEY`, else `data/outbox.jsonl` + console — the dev inbox); `tick()` each minute for lapse/freeze; every historical slug routes, and **the page it serves is live since Q391** (2026-08-19): `/` is the §9.7a birth (the creation mail is real, pasted text rides the stash), `/d/:slug` renders the real document through the page's `remoteCS` shim over `view()`, and the **engine rides every commit** (`engine-host.ts`: the bridge born at constitute, engine.jsonl + bridge.json beside the log, resumed by replay; ordinary motions race, `judge-race` in the whitelist, race cards and the wallet in the view). `npm run server` at the root. **The dev inbox is on the screen** (Ed, 2026-08-19): without RESEND_API_KEY, GET /api/dev/outbox serves the outbox tail and the page grows an off-system 📬 button (live and birth, login screen included) opening a modal of every mail with its magic link clickable — QA plays every seat without touching a console; in the production artifact the route does not exist at all — the build drops its `DEV` label bodily, so no misconfiguration can serve magic links (PRODUCTION.md stage 3, decision 437) — and the button never appears. **The operator hears about every birth** (Ed, 2026-08-20): the §9.7a save mails `cfg.notifyEmail` the title, the founder's address and the document's URL — the one mail the server sends to somebody who is not a member, and the one that does not ride the fold, since a birth is a save rather than an event anybody in the room is owed; fired and forgotten, because the save must never fail or wait on a mail. `DRAFT_NOTIFY_EMAIL` overrides, empty switches it off. **Live at docs.vote since 2026-08-20** (PRODUCTION.md stage 10, Q481a): one Render service, the alpha home, CI deploying on green (decision 476) and verifying the live host afterwards with `npm run verify`. Not in the slice (NOTES.md): chamber-public reads.
- `engine-bridge` — `packages/constitution/src/engine-bridge.ts` (367b): marries a `ConstitutionSession` to an engine-core `Session`. Ordinary set-motions race in the engine (stake from the engine wallet, rival values one race); adoption reports through `adjudicateOrdinaryMotion`; what then *stands* (applied, or parked behind a 👑) flows back by a **standing diff** in `sync(t)`, which is the ground shift, plus an engine `amend` for the bar/ending/rate/quorum/authorship; roster truth relays one way (arrive→add, lapse→suspend, return→resume, remove→remove). Deliberately not exported from index.ts so the page bundle stays engine-free — the engine runs **at the server** (Q391, `packages/server/src/engine-host.ts`): the bridge is born when the constitution settles, persists as engine.jsonl + bridge.json beside the document log (resume via the bridge's `resume`/`state()`), and the page judges through the race cards the view serves. Evidence walk: `npm run motions -w @draft/sim-harness`.
- `constitution` — **the §9 layer as a real package** (`packages/constitution`, `@draft/constitution`, built 2026-08-18 from plan 367a — the shadow-engine cure; Q334/Q335 honoured). Pure, dependency-free, browser-loadable: the 18-setting catalogue with typed values (never display strings), the consent rule as maxima under per-setting protective orders, the blind founding with live-electorate resolution and ground shifts, owed OKs, constituted, motions on both routes (constitutional unanimity settled live per v0.48; ordinary through the `adjudicateOrdinaryMotion` seam — **whose caller is real since 367b**: the `engine-bridge` races the value in engine-core and reports the verdict; only the mock still drives the seam by hand, pending Q391), the 👑 question with crown lapse and auto-pass, sign-out/freeze/lapse clocks via one host-called `tick(t)`, applications on all four rungs, hash-chained log with bit-identical `replay`. Blindness is the projection layer (`view()` — the only sanctioned member read path); answers ride events in plaintext (NOTES.md records why commit-reveal was rejected). Ships a pure-TS sha256 (node:crypto parity asserted, lone surrogates included); engine-core adopted its twin in 367b, so the engine no longer needs node either. **The browser bundle is committed** (`design/constitution.js`, esbuild IIFE `window.CONSTITUTION`, exact-pinned 0.28.2, config single-sourced in `bundle.config.mjs`, byte-freshness test; rebuild: `npm run bundle`). `npm run founding -w @draft/sim-harness` walks it deterministically with a narrative log. Author calls live in `packages/constitution/NOTES.md`.
- `sim-harness` — synthetic-participant simulator driving engine-core; parameter sweeps against throughput, stability, bridge rate, backlog quality. `npm run evidence -w @draft/sim-harness` runs the deferred-question studies (deterministic, no network); findings live in packages/sim-harness/REPORT-deferred-evidence.md.
- `participant-api` — the blind-discipline surface (engine-core module) every participant speaks: cards, judge, submit, gazette, browse. Humans, sim personas, and personal AIs are interchangeable behind it.
- `scripted-persona` / `llm-persona` — deterministic utility-model participants (regression + welfare ground truth) vs claude-haiku-4-5 participants (realism).
- `welfare-ratio` — sim metric: (achieved − incumbent) / (optimal − incumbent) summed utility over the roster; 1.0 = utilitarian-best text found.

## Reversals worth keeping (2026-08-21)

- **"Untitled" in the birth heading, restored the evening it was removed.** That morning the heading was blanked until a title was set, on the argument that *Untitled* was helper text standing where the answer goes — the one place this surface never puts a suggestion. That evening Ed watched a user tester **type the document's title into the prose column underneath the empty heading**, and asked for it back. The argument had misidentified the object: the answer goes on the 🪶 card, and the heading is where the *result* appears, so "Untitled" is an honest statement of the result so far rather than a suggestion competing with a lane. A heading with nothing in it gives the eye nothing to fix on, and the eye goes looking for the nearest writable thing. Kept as a reversal rather than folded silently, because the losing argument is a good argument that a single observation beat — which is the whole case for testing surfaces on people.

- **Acknowledgment gates the power, not the receipt.** The grant tasks (Q448/453) were built so that no power arrives without acknowledgment, but the acknowledgment reached only three `style.display` lines — the ✏️ ✒️ 🏛️ wallets. Everything a power actually *does* was gated on the module's own `can*` predicates, which go true at 🍾. So between the press and the OK a member held every power with no wallet to show for it: the receipt was hidden and the power ran. Ed's rule (*I should not be able to do actions until I have acknowledged that I have been given the right to do them*) makes the OK the conferring act rather than a notification about one. What made this cheap to get wrong: the wallet is the visible half, so gating it *looks* like gating the power, and every fixture walk presses the OKs in order and never observes the window in between.

## Conventions

- Windows machine, PowerShell 5.1 — see global CLAUDE.md traps (no Get-Content/Set-Content rewrites, use Bash tool for gh/multi-line git).
- **Checking a mockup:** serve `design/` over localhost (a tiny node http server) and measure the DOM with the browser tools — geometry, computed styles, element counts. `file://` URLs are refused by the extension. Two traps cost real time on 2026-08-16: the automation tab runs **backgrounded**, so `requestAnimationFrame` never fires, CSS transitions don't run, `:hover` never applies, and `setTimeout` is clamped to ~1s. Anything animated must be driven by stubbing the animation helper, and hover states can only be verified by reading the CSS rule. A stalled sequence in that tab usually means rAF, not a bug.
- **Mockup fixtures:** one array of items, each carrying its own content, progress and state — never parallel literals kept in sync by hand. Content is hand-authored; `sim-harness` has no session-state exporter, and the fixture holds diffs, rationales and captions the engine has no opinion about anyway.
- No deploys or pushes without Ed's say-so.

## Spec pass 2 (2026-08-22, Q585–624)

The second pass lifted the rest of the surface grammar out of the glossary: SURFACE.md §6 (the marks alphabet and the rail), §7 (the wallets, sockets and holds), §8 (the founding order and the band), §9 (the card kinds, the commit row, the composer); STYLE.md §8 took the card copy rules. CLAUDE.md keeps one-line pointers. The rulings that were not mere drift:

- **🏛️ arrives with the first blind question you are asked** (Q605, superseding Q514's *at arrival*). The build had it at 🍾 through ⚖️'s task, so the founding answers — which the four-verbs rule calls a kind of 🏛️ — were made with a voice nobody had been handed. *At arrival* was right for an invitee into a founding with open questions and wrong for a founder who delegates nothing; the voice now precedes the first act it licenses, whoever you are.
- **↻ is grey** (Q612). It had kept the accent blue from the emoji era; blue is *yours*, and a void judgment is information — nothing is asked of you until the pair is re-served.
- **The ⚔️ desk's 🗑️ is never disabled** (Q613); the sealed record, the 👑 question, the door's and the applicant's email cards keep their own shapes as recorded exceptions (Y20).
- **A motion's ✏️ Propose is a hold with the pencil flight** (Q614). It had been a click from before the hold ladder existed; it spends a ✏️, and every spend is held.
- **The power tabs are two yes/no questions** (Q615): *Can the Founder Make Amendments at Will?* / *Does the Founder Have a Veto?* — Ed's wording for the pen, replacing *Who Has the Pen?* so the pair reads as one grammar.
- **🍾's clause heads Proposals** while its task is last (Q616, Y21): it heads the section because it is what opens it.
- **🤝 stands above the members list** (Q617) — *I need to decide Applications before I can do Membership*; the contrary sentence of the same day is struck.
- **The 🌍 clause states the interim rule while undecided** (Q618): *Until the Founder decides, only members can see the document.* A clause that says only *is deciding* states that nothing has been decided, not what applies meanwhile.
- **A member's answer tasks ride `ORDER`** (Q619 b), retiring the last use of the wave graph's `dep` arrays — the founder's single file and the member's are one file.
- **One label per rung, everywhere** (Q620), with Ed's wordings; the composer filters the standing rule by value, not by label text, which was the fragility that made the mismatch dangerous.
- **The shield flies out at the close like the pen** (Q621). **The ✒️ commit under an unacknowledged pen stays dark and names where the pen is** (Q622, Y19). **Setup rail entries take the urgency ramp** (Q623). **Two rules absorbed four exceptions** (Q624).
- **The checker asserts everything the pass made tabular, the headless founding walk included** (Q586 c) — to be re-asked on 2026-08-29 (Q625). **The notes files are superseded, kept as history** (Q587).

## The glossary as it stood, 2026-08-23 (Q725)

The third extraction from `CLAUDE.md` reduced every name entry to a name, a one-line job and
pointers. This is the section it replaced, verbatim and in full, so that nothing removed from the
operative file is only in git: the rules it restated now live in `SPEC.md`, `SURFACE.md` and
`design/STYLE.md`, and the post-mortems moved to CLAUDE.md's own *Gotchas* section rather than
here — those twenty-one bullets are the one part of the old section that is absent below, because
they are still live above.

**CLAUDE.md is operative; this is the record.** Where the two disagree, CLAUDE.md wins.

## Glossary — named parts

Use these names in all discussion, commits, and code. Literal and stable beats elegant.

**Retired names**, kept here so a lookup lands: `document-creation`, `founding-ceremony` and `setup.html` (the surfaces) → `session-view.html`; `edit-wallet` → `propose-wallet`; `setup-piles` → `constitution-section`; `drafting-desk`, `arrival-bar`, `bounty-board`, `redline` → gone. Their reasoning is in `design/DECISIONS.md`.

**Engine (mechanism, no UI):**
- `engine-core` — session state machine: candidates, races, adoptions, the adoption-threshold ramp, tokens/refunds, adoption floors, certification. Caught up through 367(b): quorum rides the floor (§4.2), the drip is real minutes (`tokenDripMinutes`), amendments bind races in flight (`amend`/`standing-set`, §4.3 anchor re-seating — **a bar never jumps**), suspension is lapse engine-side (§9.5a), **setting races** carry ordinary motions (a candidate is a patch *or* a typed value; adoption of a value is a verdict, applied only by the host's `setStanding`, which is also the ground shift). The hash is pure TS — the engine is node-free and browser-loadable.
  **Adoptions batch on the cooldown metronome** (§4.2): when the cooldown from the last batch elapses, every race clearing threshold+floor adopts at once — the document changes at most once per cooldown, by as much as the room decided. One decision per race per batch (the ready set snapshots before anything lands), oldest first, each rebasing the next; **a race adopts only once somebody has actually judged in it**. `Session.tick(t)` is the host clock; the bridge's `tick(t)` rides it and the server's minute timer drives the bridge, so a due batch lands even in a quiet room. The cooldown is **engine tuning, never constitutional**, and a cleared race is never surfaced as waiting — no ⏳, since deriving "cleared and cooling" would be a standings reveal.
- `adoption-threshold` — the confidence bar a challenger's win-probability must clear; ramps over the session window (wall clock). Formerly "θ"; always use this name.
- `patch-engine` — text machinery: diffs, footprints, three-way merge, rebase, surgery.
- `overlap-gates` — the three-gate classifier for colliding patches: textual composition → semantic composition → rivalry.
- `ranking-model` — Bradley–Terry preference model (per race) + global salience model, with active pair sampling and deadlock detection.
- `router` — v/c_p feed ordering, hot set, exploration/salience streams, floors-aware serving, notification digests.
- `event-log` — append-only hash-chained log, seeded RNG, participant receipts.
- `dedup-gate` — submission-time duplicate check (embeddings, edit distance, LLM equivalence) plus behavioral dedup probes.
- `race-labeler` — advisory naming and typing of disputes: a name ("treasurer oversight") and a type (copy-edit · substantive · structural) per race, from the oracle's `describeRace`, with a deterministic nearest-heading + excerpt fallback. Outside the state machine — labels never enter the event log or gate anything; type is stored but not wired to routing (Q49).
- `document-modes` — two **independent** settings, both fixed at creation (SPEC §9.0). **Window**: an end datetime, or none (perpetual) — one question with one answer, which is why the surface asks it on one card (⏰ *When does it end?*) with *never* as an option. **Threshold shape**: fixed, or ramping. A windowed document may hold its threshold fixed; only a perpetual one is *constrained*, since a ramp needs an endpoint to ramp toward. A window gives you the closing ceremony and a drip paced against it; without one the drip runs against real time and the document ends by `freeze`.
- `founding-ceremony` — the stage before any drafting where the membership sets quorum and threshold: each member states, blind, the *lowest they will accept*, and the document takes the **maximum** of each. A consent rule rather than a vote, which is what lets it escape the constitutional bootstrap — the result satisfies every stated minimum by construction. Distribution published without names (SPEC §9.0a).
- `sign-out` — a member declaring they are done, choosing **holding** (they stay in the quorum base, so enough of them freezes the document — *I do not consent to you finishing without me*) or **abstaining** (they leave the base — *I trust you to finish up*). Plain silence is never sign-out; judgments already cast keep counting afterwards; the base is the whole roster minus abstainers, never the active remainder (SPEC §9.5).
- `freeze` — what happens when active plus holding falls below quorum: races park, participants are notified, the record is cut. A stall with an alarm, not a death — it thaws if enough return, and it is the perpetual mode's equivalent of a close.
- `coherence-auditor` — machine drafter patrolling document drift, fixed token budget. **Not a member**: it never judges and counts toward no quorum — it only proposes, and its proposals compete like anybody's. Surface card: **AI Proposals** 🤖, in the **Proposals** section beside the grant and the drip. **Ordinary**: defaults to the founder, judging never waits on it, a motion on it is a race; delegating it to the room stays the founder's option, most-restrictive answer winning.

**Design system** (session-view's, tokenised — every surface adopts it rather than inventing its own):
- `cards.js` — **the decision-card grammar, one implementation.** `window.CARDS`: a pure layer (the diff/markdown engine, `resultOnly`, `speakerHtml`, `fieldHtml`, `fieldOf`, the drawn TICK/CROSS and MARK glyphs) and a `make(env)` factory (lane bars, proposal blocks, `clauseHeadHtml`, commit row, `laneBoxHtml`, the open/close geometry, `keepStill`).
  **Env keys are functions read at call time** — session-view's `topUrgentId` is a reassigned `let`, its `chilled` a mutable Set, so nothing may be captured at `make()` time. Seams: `o.marks`/`o.washAttrs` on the clause head (the setup strip arrives pre-wrapped; session's chips/wash inject unchanged), `valAttr` (session radios speak `data-v`, setup composers `data-mval`, motion judging `data-motion`), a bindable speaker tooltip.
  **The contamination guard**: `design/reference/` holds byte-frozen copies of the whole page set (tag `post-merge` since 2026-08-21; `pre-cards` and `pre-constitution` before it) — **never edit; re-freeze on intentional change**, and check it has not rotted: the pre-constitution copy had drifted 200 diffs from HEAD before anybody noticed. `design/tools/session-probe.js` walks all 43 cards on `session-view.html?fixture=session` against the frozen one (whitespace-normalized outerHTML equality per card, plus 0.0px geometry). **Re-run it whenever cards.js changes, with both runs at one window size and both pages scrolled to 0** — a restored scroll position on one side reads as a constant offset in every rect.
  **The open/close animation uses `clip-path: inset(-60px -100px 0 -100px)`, never `overflow: hidden`** — the tab strip hangs entirely outside the card's box (`right: 100%`) and gets clipped for the whole motion otherwise. Related: the open paragraph needs `display: flow-root`, or margin collapse silently defeats fitBand's head-rule correction.
- `wash` — the lifecycle colour on any surface that carries one: a clause, a `clause-head`, a held-open gap, a queue entry. **Colour changes fade** rather than jump, over `--wash-ms` (700ms). Two things make that work: a queue entry's wash is painted as a **bar** (a `::before` whose width is the fill, `background-color` the hue) rather than a hard-stopped gradient, since **no browser interpolates between two gradients**; and because both rails are rebuilt wholesale on every render, each washed element is rendered wearing its **previous** colour and handed the new one after one forced reflow — **keyed by what the wash is *about***, so a clause and the head of the card that swallows it share one key and opening a card deepens the colour rather than repainting it.
- `room-pulse` — one grey dot in the topbar that beats once per action by anybody, yourself included. Deliberately **content-free**: no count, no direction, no author, so the only thing it can say is *the room is moving* — which is what keeps it inside SPEC §3.5, since a heartbeat with nothing in it cannot leak standings. Grey at rest and grey at its brightest; it beats by growing a ring, never by changing colour. In the mockup the other members are a timer on a fixed *irregular* cycle — irregular so it does not read as a machine, fixed so it can be QA'd.
- `me` — the user avatar at the right of the topbar. Initials by default. It is the only face on the surface, since everybody else's authorship is sealed (SPEC §3.4) — Q278 asks whether that stays true.
- `lifecycle palette` — **three** hues held as raw RGB channels: `--lc-open` 🔥💡⚔️ yellow, `--lc-weigh` 🌶️ pink, `--lc-yours` ✏️ **blue** (the accent), and one grey shared by `--lc-deciding` ⏳ and `--lc-closed` ✔✖↻. Two rules govern it:
  - **Grey means nothing is being asked of you** (not "you can no longer affect it" — a ⏳ race is revisable until it seals).
  - **Hot for actions, cold for information** — yellow and pink want something from you; blue and grey tell you where things stand.
  🔥 takes a **fixed alpha clear of the ramp's ceiling** (`FLAME_A`, 0.44) with the urgency ramp running underneath it, because 🔥 means *the question you are being asked next* — a category, and a ramp cannot express a category. Applied identically to the queue card's wash, the paragraph's wash and the gutter mark. **`--ok`'s green never leaves a card interior**; every *decided* state washes grey in a margin, and the two must not meet (Q264).
- `--primary` — the one accent: every wire, every selection, every control that is on. `--ok` — green, meaning *decided* and only that.
- type scale `--t-lead / --t-body / --t-ui / --t-small / --t-cap / --t-micro` — **and every piece of text on the surface is on it.** One exception is allowed to be a literal: a **glyph** size (`.achip` 1.125rem, the glyph buttons 1.2/1.35rem, the serif italic *I* 1.05rem), which tunes a silhouette to a box rather than text to a scale. The `.eyebrow` class carries the single upper-case label treatment at `--t-micro` and **names its users in its own selector** (`.headlab`, `.fieldlab`, `.rtag`, `.rechead`, `.pwhere`); each user keeps colour and layout and nothing else. A shared rule nothing points at is not a shared rule.
- spacing `--s1`–`--s5` on a 4px grid; radii `--r-sm / --r-md / --r-lg`; elevation `--shadow-sm/md/lg/xl`, **neutral, never tinted** — a drop shadow rather than a glow, each step a 1px contact shadow plus one soft cast whose blur stays near 1.6× its offset. Layout constants `--rail-left / --rail-right / --nav-h`.

**Product (UI and founding):**
- `session-view.html` — **the one surface** (stage 8, Q418 (a), 2026-08-21): `design/session-view.html`, one page from blank arrival through the founding to a live, drafted document. `setup.html`, `document-creation.html` and `founding-ceremony.html` are redirects. *The bullets below still say "this surface" about the founding half; the charter half is the `session-view` entry.*
  - **The merge**: the page keeps setup's live wiring (`S`, `ctx`, `remoteCS`, the 4s poll) and mounts SESSION at **`#charter`** under the band once the text is confirmed (`.doc.begun` hides the founder's pre-confirm editor `#prose`). **One block is one engine line**: `blocksOf(text)` keys each line `L<i>` (a `# ` prefix is a heading), so a proposal on a paragraph is exactly one hunk and no mapping layer stands between the page and the engine's footprint. **One rail**: setup tasks are SESSION entries (`env.extraEntries`) laid out by `layoutQueue` beside their own band paragraphs — ask, news and yours pin, wait flows — fading like every other entry. **One wire layer, one TOC** (the constitution leads), **one open card** (band and charter arbitrate both ways). Live: `itemsFromView(v)` builds the items from the blind view (text races as quick/race cards, `mine`, the record with OK persisted in `localStorage` per document and member); `LIVE_HOOKS` post `judge-race` / `propose-text` / `withdraw-text`; a race whose candidates are all mine asks nothing; a stale base returns the draft unproposed with the refusal in its foot; the wallet shows the engine's balance and the tray invents no time the server does not serve (Q503); live races carry no fill (Q501). **Fixture modes**: no query = the founding fixture; `?fixture=session` = the Hollow Oak session (band hidden; `&band=1` shows the composition). It is `session-view` with nothing in it yet — same three columns, same rails, same card-over-document gesture — because the settings are not a form, they are **a queue of things that want you**, which is what that rail already means.
  - **The birth is its own phase, with its own layout** (Ed, 2026-08-21: *the Birth is really a custom phase and has different layout logic to the document after it's created*). Four rules, all keyed on `cs` being absent. **No heading**: the group's label is empty pre-save, so no **Constitution** heading and no fold control stand over it (Ed, 2026-08-21) — nothing is being headed yet, and the contents rail already withholds its Constitution entry until the save, so a heading here disagreed with the outline beside it. A group with no label draws no `.pilelab`, which keeps the phase knowledge on the surface rather than in `setup.js`. **No sections**: the band's constitution group takes `sections: cs ? secLive() : []`, because before the save there is no constitution to divide up — the run is the title, the link and 📧, all three in the group's own `lead`. The band used to derive its sections from whatever had a visible card, and 📧 is a Membership key, so a *Membership* heading, a *Members* subheading and an empty list stood over a document that did not exist. **📧 stands in the opening run for the whole birth** — open, unsent and awaiting the link — since it used to be drawn by the Membership body while open and by the `lead` once sent, so the paragraph jumped up the page at the send and the tab moved. **And the sentences are third person like every other clause**: `decisionLine` gives 📧 *The Founder is checking their email for a link.* once the mail is out, and only after the save the members-list "you" (*Your address is verified.*), which is the sanctioned exception and belongs to your own member row. **Before the mail goes out the clause is blank** (Ed, 2026-08-21) — the tab alone, standing where its sentence will appear, the way an `insert-anchor` is a held-open gap with its mark beside it; nothing has been decided, and a document does not narrate that somebody is about to type. The paragraph still holds its tab's own height, so nothing collapses and the tab keeps one position blank, open and sent. The group's note counted the three feathers pre-save; derived from sections it had no work to count and read *all confirmed* over an unfinished birth. (**The note itself went on 2026-08-25** — Ed: *there's some helper text in the document that is like "n still to confirm or answer", please remove this*, and then the *all confirmed* state and the pre-save feathers with it. All three states are gone, so the birth's own count is a solved problem that no longer exists; Q684, which asked what the note counted, closes with it. Q783.) The rail still enumerates from `secLive()` — that is what keeps the birth's own tasks in it.
  - **The birth order is fixed** (Q460, Ed 2026-08-20; built 2026-08-21): **🪶 title → 📍 link → 📧 your email**, then the constitution in its own order (see `setup-queue`), each card born as the one before settles. The link comes before the email so that **all three feathers are spent in one sitting**, before the founder leaves for their inbox, and so that **the magic link is the instantiation**: nothing exists anywhere until it is followed. The address is collected for real — checked live against `GET /api/slug/:slug`, **reserved on send for the pending stash's TTL** (Q462b: a second founder asking for it gets *that address is taken* and a suggestion; the reservation is the one oracle on pending documents, the price of promising an address), and honoured at the click. The quill line (the save) has not moved — the save has moved to the click. Arrival presents a brand-new *unsaved* document whose rail holds one task, 🪶 Title. **Nothing on this surface opens itself, arrival included** — opening a card is always the user's click, because they must learn that tasks and tabs are clicked.
  - **The email is verified before anything is saved.** The mail (*you have named a document “[title]” and chosen its address, docs.vote/[slug] — open this link to create it there*) is itself the login, so clicking it proves the address and creates the document at the promised address. While nothing exists a typo costs nothing. **Following the mail is what saves the document** — until then nothing exists anywhere and nobody can be invited, because an invitation *is* the link (SPEC §9.7a). Sybil wariness at this door is parked as Q346.
  - Answering the title spawns the 📍 link card pre-filled with a slug from the title, checked available as you type (the fixture keeps a `TAKEN` set; live asks the server), a taken one offering the suffixed suggestion. **At the live birth the verification mail is real** and the `mail-modal` never opens over it — the modal is fixture furniture, and the 📧 card's sent state says where the mail actually is.
  - **A card with a dependency does not appear until its dependency is settled** — never greyed; a gate or grant stands alongside rather than waiting (F3, F9). Pasted text: markdown hashes do not survive the paste (this is the column the document will be edited in, and session-view has no markdown in it), and a leading `# Title` becomes the title and leaves the document.
  - Live constraints: perpetual forces a fixed threshold, and the drip is per-hour.
  - **Who you are is the dev dropdown**, top-left under the topbar — deliberately off the design system (dashed, monospace: a stagehand, not a member of the cast), listing the actual users: the founder, each person as invited, an applicant, a stranger. Per-user state lives **on the roster rows**; `S.myAns`/`S.okd`/`S.draft`/`S.mAns`/`S.myname…` are property accessors delegating to whoever is in the chair, so every existing predicate renders every seat unchanged. Beside it, **⏩ settle the founding** completes the founding in one press (two members invited, every answer filled *coherently with the founder defaults* so all the surface's sentences agree, gates open).
  - **Motions gate on `canJudge()`** (§9.6a — the founder re-sets freely before the start, so the composer appears only once the constitution is settled). **What stands on a delegated setting is derived live from the room's answers** (`takenOf`/`standsOf`, reading the module's consent resolution), never the founder's field, which only says who holds it.
  - **The composers are session-view's own**: lanes are cards.js `laneBarHtml` speaking `data-mval`; the rationale is the editing-card's sealed-disc speaker with `data-placeholder`, **never an avatar**, since authorship is sealed. The PROPOSE map covers **every** composable setting, so nothing falls through to a bare text box. One `cardFor` renders ordinary and constitutional motion cards, the withdraw-returns-whole rule and per-viewer judged tallies.
  - **The page renders over `@draft/constitution`**: a `ConstitutionSession` born at the save (§9.7a) holds settled values, delegations, blind counts, resolutions, gates, motions and the register; the page keeps rendering, its copy, and the **provisional layer** only (radio positions, unsent answers, a motion being typed — the `S` fields are exactly that and nothing more).
  - **Three addresses, one page**: served from the product server it detects its address — `/` is the birth, `/d/:slug` a real document through `remoteCS` (a ConstitutionSession-lookalike over the blind `view()`: reads from the projection, commands as serialized POSTs folded under the cookie's identity, values re-hydrated on a 4s poll **but never under the open card**, dev chrome hidden, the ordinary judgment a real `judge-race`). Opened as a file or from the static design server it is the fixture.
  - **Contamination guard**: `design/tools/setup-probe.js` against `design/reference/session-view.html` (tag `refs-founded-2026-08-22`) — 42 steps, no allowances after a fresh freeze; the *Founded at [time]* minute can fire on `band` across a minute boundary, and a rerun is clean.
  - Open: ordinary adjudication in the page is still a **dev seam** (all-judged majority). The real adjudicator is `engine-bridge`; an ordinary motion should be served as a pairwise race card, not an everyone-votes tally.
  - Naming note: **`draft` already means a candidate patch** everywhere in this project, so the thing this surface makes is a *document* or a *charter*, never a draft.
- `setup-queue` — the right-hand rail. **The five states, the founding order (`ORDER`), what blocks and what hides are SURFACE §6 and §8**; the checker asserts the order table against `ORDER` and `SEC`. Seams worth knowing: `blocksOrder` in session-view.html is where the pen is the one blocking grant; `orderReady` consults nothing but `ORDER` for the keys it names; a member's `ans-*` tasks ride the same order (Q619); `ansDue` is the *unless nothing else is outstanding* exception (Q408/Q413) — and **a gate or a grant is never one of the other tasks**, because `otherTasksLeft` is what that exception reads and two gates were outstanding precisely *because* the answers were: 🍾 is outstanding by definition until pressed and the module refuses the press while a question collects, and 🏛️ is the grant that arrives **with** the first question asking you (Q605), so it was withholding the very thing it accompanies. Between them the exception was unreachable and a founder who founded in one sitting arrived at Begin, was refused, and had nowhere to answer (Q645). The rule is `askingFirst`'s own, generalised: *what a question hands you cannot be the reason to withhold the question* — ordering is kept by `ORDER`/`blocksOrder`, never by counting news. `npm run founder-answers` walks it (`founding-walk.mjs --delegate=chamber`) and **asserts the question was offered, not that it is still pending** — the founder answers it the moment it arrives, so the final rail is empty on a working surface. It cannot ride `journey`: a delegated question never resolves on one voice, so `begin` refuses and the live walk would correctly stall short of a begun document. The old default set survives in exactly one place, ⏩'s `SEED`, so the stagehand still settles into the Hollow Oak document the fixtures describe. `CHOSEN`, per setting, is what keeps every commit dark until there is an answer. Full walk-through in `design/setup.notes.md` (superseded, and deleted 2026-08-23; `git show aba22fd:design/setup.notes.md`); residuals in Q350.
  - `birth-pass` — **what is born arrives, it does not appear.** Both rails and the whole band are rebuilt wholesale on every render, so nothing on screen has an identity across one; `birthPass` (setup.js) keys each element by what it is *about* (`data-para`, section id, `data-q`), remembers every key ever seen, and hands a newborn its full presence after one forced reflow. Two entrances, by what the element does to its neighbours: a **rail entry grows from zero height while it fades** (so neighbours are part of the motion rather than jumping in one frame); a **document paragraph or section only fades** (text that slides while you read it is worse than text that arrives). A batch **cascades in document order**, 55ms apart, capped at six, each column counting its own. **The two lanes are paced differently, and deliberately**: the document lane fades over `FADE_MS`, **840ms since Ed doubled it on 2026-08-21** — a clause arriving in the band is text to be read, and at 420ms it went by at the pace of a control answering a press — while the rail keeps `GROW_MS` 240ms, because there an entry grows from zero height and its neighbours move with it, so it is paced against the movement rather than against reading. Section headings ride the document lane and slow with the clauses. The stagger is not part of it; a card's own crossfades (170ms) are not either. Reduced motion fades everything and moves nothing; a stagehand act (seat switch, ⏩) mutes the pass rather than replaying fifteen entrances at once.
  - `card-morph` — **switching cards within one strip is a morph, not a teardown.** The box survives: its height glides from the old card's to the new card's while the body crossfades, and the head — where the strip lives — swaps in place, so the tab you clicked never blinks and never moves (0.0px on both axes). From anywhere else the old card still closes into its own paragraph first and the new one opens out of its, because there the document *would* jump by a card's height in one frame. `clip-path`, never `overflow: hidden` — the strip hangs outside the card's box.
  - `dead-click-nudge` — **a click that lands on nothing beats the task that is waiting** (SURFACE F17, Q650–Q653). During the birth only, with no card open, a click on document space carrying no control lifts and settles *both* the next task's tab and its rail entry (`task-beat`, 640ms, two beats). It exists because `#doctitle` was a bare `<h1>` with no `data-card` and nothing listening above it, so the founder's first instinct — edit the title in place — was answered with **nothing at all**, and the tester Ed watched went and typed the title into the prose column instead. Four things to know. **Both halves beat, and that is the lesson**: the tab, the rail entry and the card are one thing, where beating either alone only points at a corner of the screen. **It never opens the card**, however often it is clicked — C1/F10 keep the opening click the user's, and the nudge stays a teacher. **Nothing beats unprompted**: the intermittent jiggle first asked for was rejected, because every other motion here is a consequence of something and the birth has no clock to ride anyway (the 4s poll is live-mode only). And **the transform sits on `.queue button`, never on `.qitem`** — the `li` is what `layoutQueue` positions, what `drawWires` measures the cable's start from, and what setup-probe measures to 0.01px, so a moving `li` would leave its cable behind *and* flake the probe on every birth step; the class rides the `li` (whose `class` attribute the probe strips) while the CSS reaches its button. Dead is structural — no `a/button/input/textarea/select/label/[role=button]/[contenteditable=true]` ancestor — rather than a list of `data-*` hooks that would rot. `#prose` is excluded deliberately (Q654). Reduced motion holds the lift instead of moving; the class comes off on a `setTimeout` as well as `animationend`, since under reduced motion there is no animation to end.
- `commit-row grammar` — **discard, commit, and OK**: SURFACE §9.1. Two code rules inside it: what-it-stands-as is **remembered, not derived** — one snapshot per card, living until that card commits, never one per opening, since provisional values survive a close; and a revert restores **only the keys the open card can write**, read off its own controls (`data-set`/`data-txt`/`data-num`/`data-ans`). **The ground belongs to the glyph, never to the card's kind** — `commitGlyph` chooses by era, and one expression decides both glyph and ground.
  - `setup-band` — **the constitution, at the top of the document.** A decision card replaces the clause it is about; a setting is about the whole document, so it opens where the whole document begins — in the document, never over it. It draws a `queue-wire` to its rail entry and carries the same lifecycle wash, and a settled setting leaves a grey tab, so the screen ends in a tab group at the head of the document, next to the title: one grey tab per rule the room agreed.
- `gate-cards` — 💡 **Proposing** and ⚖️ **Judging**: not settings — nobody decided them, they follow from the others — but exactly the shape of an unacknowledged decision, so they take its rule: hidden until 🍾 (Y5), news-green with a drawn ✔ and an OK the moment they open, gone from the rail once pressed. 💡 is also the ✏️ grant (Q461a). What each waits on is drawn as the cards themselves.
- `motion-controls` — **a motion is composed with the setting's own control**, never a free-text lane, and **the settled card IS the composer** (SURFACE §9.2, K1–K7). `PROPOSE` in session-view.html covers every composable setting — the checker asserts it equals the composable set — and filters the standing rule by value, never by label text (Q620). 📄 composes nowhere.
- `reserved` / 👑 — **the two powers are ✒️ drafting (changing the setting directly) and 🛡️ assent (a member-carried change waits on the founder's accept)** — a philosophical sword and shield: one writes, one only refuses. They are held and relinquished **separately and one-way**; a `reserve` motion restores either or both (Q394). *Code still carries the older names `unilateral` and `assent`; the surface has worn ✒️/🛡️ since the glyph batch of 2026-08-21 (Q454), and 🔧 has left the vocabulary.*
  - **👑 is the mark beside a person** who holds either power anywhere; **📯** the mark of one who holds none. The half-states have characters: **pen without shield** — a founder who *does* things (the membership: they invite alone, carried applications need nobody); **shield without pen** — a pure veto, the constitutional monarch (the threshold).
  - **A founder who reserves nothing and is not a member has no role at all** — every post-start founder power flows through reservation, so at zero they are a name in the record (SPEC §9.7). The membership can return reservations to them; that is their one standing property.
  - **Timing**: delegation keeps its own clock; **either power goes once the setting has a value**, and a release made before the start is recorded then and takes effect at 🍾 (SPEC §9.7 rule 3 → why: R-048, Ed 2026-08-25). Until that ruling the two ran on two calendars — assent from creation, drafting waiting for the text to confirm — which is the wording every note before this line still carries. Pre-start the pair is as revisable as any radio (§9.6a — `reclaim` restores a relinquished power, pending or spent, without touching its value).
  - **Reserved is assent, not silence.** Anybody may motion a reserved setting, by its own kind's route, which reservation never alters; a change the membership passes goes to the founder as a **👑 question** — theirs to accept or reject, **at the end of either route**. It is a task of its own kind (news-pinned, Refuse / Accept its commit row — the Founder's words).
  - **The crown lapses like a member** (§9.5a's clock runs on the founder too), and **lapse is automatic abstention**: assent grants itself while the crown sleeps, nothing changes hands, and revival — just logging in — restores the requirement. A pending 👑 question at crown lapse **passes automatically**. What keeps a monarchy mortal is that an absent king can refuse nothing.
  - **The verb is delegate, and it is never a button**: "The title is delegated to the members" is a constitutional sentence among the setting's own options, preferred and committed like any value. The founder may delegate anything they hold from the moment proposing opens. One-way; **the road back is a constitutional `reserve` motion that lands without the founder's assent**, since the release from an unwanted crown is delegation itself — which is also why the room can crown a **lapsed** founder.
  - Built in `@draft/constitution` (`SettingState.powers`, `relinquish`). The founder's direct hand on a card **is** the drafting power (`founderDirect`), so an assent-only setting renders the founder the member composer; the composer's carried-to-👑 note keys on the assent power (`crownWaits`), so a drafting-only setting truthfully promises no crown question. The register's pair lives inside the applications value (`reserved` / `reserved-unilateral` / `reserved-assent` / `members`).
  - Out of scope by Ed's word: reserving the text (Q387); individual delegation, foundership transfer, inheritance (Q388 — nobody inherits a lapsed crown). Residuals in Q349.
- `governance-tabs` — **every held-able setting is a tab group**: the value's own tab in front, and beneath it two power tabs — ✒️ *Can the Founder Make Amendments at Will?* and 🛡️ *Does the Founder Have a Veto?* (Q615) Drawn with the existing pile machinery (`ctx.chipsFor` names the pile in setup.html; setup.js's `para` renders it and opens any member of the group at the setting's own paragraph with the whole group as the strip). A power card's head is that power's rule as it stands (`powerHeadLine`), its body the give-up radios (`powerBody`), its commit `commitPower` — revisable pre-start (reclaim-then-redrop), one-way after.
  **Delegation is the state of holding neither power** — and since Q511 (Ed, 2026-08-21) it is also **an option on the decision card**: *Let the membership decide this*, set apart after the values because it is not one of them, and doubling as the only way past a question you are not ready to answer in a single-file founding. `delegateRung` renders it once for whatever card is open (so no body can forget it and no undelegable setting can get it); `theyDecide` still renders the members-hold note on a setting already handed over, and picking a value is the taking-back. Module seam: pre-start, giving up the second power on a delegable setting emits `setting-delegated`, opening the blind founding question.
  **The constitution states governance by deviation under a preamble**: the opening paragraph states the defaults once (anyone may propose; ✏️ at the threshold, 🏛️ by consent; nobody drafts directly, nothing waits on assent, unless a rule says so), and `holderLine` appends **only the deviations** — a delegated clause says nothing.
  Truth rule: a members-held setting whose value stands (a post-start hand-over) states its value like any settled rule — the "n of E have answered" line keys on the module's `collecting`, **never on fixture progress**. Since Q506 and Q440 (2026-08-21) **every** held-able setting is in the pattern, 🤝 Applications (its value is the join policy alone; the pair left the value) and 📄 the Text included — a founder who keeps ✒️ or 🛡️ on the Text is a 👑 like anywhere else, and 🛡️ on the Text means an adoption waits on their accept.
- `task` — the general term for *a thing tracked by sidebar cards that opens a document card with the controls*; a **card** is the UX element representing it. Copy says tasks; the design system names the objects cards; "queue-card" never appears in anything a member reads.
- `applications-card` — 🤝 **Applications**, split from 🪪 **Membership**: the register card keeps who is in and the inviting; the policy card holds **who holds the membership** and the **join policy**. Constitutional, on both surfaces; its band label follows its own holder radio. SPEC §9.7½.
- `applicants` — a subsection of Membership: the people asking to be in the room. Hangs off the 🤝 card's **join policy**, four rungs: *invitation only* · *applications must be proposed* (a member proposes it, staking the ✏️ — **a second is a proposed application**) · *anyone may apply* (straight to the threshold, free — the tasks its price, the threshold its filter) · *open*.
  Where application is allowed, a non-member reader's rail holds one task, Apply for membership, which spawns four: 📧 **an email, verified by magic link before anything can be submitted** — the applicant's **identity**, and **member emails are unique** (one address, one member; an address already on the membership is told to log in instead, which also kills Q346's double-invitation vector) — then ✋ name, 🖼️ picture, 📝 a few optional words. **An empty application is a real application**; nothing is collected before Begin, nothing sent before Submit.
  A submitted application becomes an **ordinary motion to grant membership** — deliberately a different door from invitation-by-consent (the policy is the constitutional act, each application its ordinary consequence). **Each admit motion is its own one-candidate race against the membership as it stands** — a synthetic per-applicant engine setting (`admit:<id>`), which is what makes two applicants *structurally* unable to be raced against each other. The author is the seconder under *proposed* (✏️ staked) or the applicant themself under *apply* (their words the rationale; a voice in the engine for exactly that act, suspended out of E in the same breath).
  **Every proposal to bring somebody in offers a rationale** — the lane is always in the composer and the case rides the motion — but **a blank rationale is a real proposal**; the module never refuses one. Live, members meet applicants in the **Applicants block** on the open 🪪 card (name, words, Admit / Keep the membership as it is / Indifferent + ✓ riding `judge-race`). The applicant's whole view is the setup surface's applicant seat. SPEC §9.7½.
- `lapse` — 💤 *Do memberships lapse?*: whether an **inactive** membership lapses (the surface says "inactive", never "quiet") and after how long, each member stating the **shortest quiet spell** they would accept and the document taking the longest, *never* the longest of all. A lapsed member leaves the quorum base like an abstainer, keeps their cast judgments counted, is warned by email first, receives **the package** (the document as it stands + the record to date) at the lapse, and revives by simply logging in — consented rule, no motion. SPEC §9.5a.
- `mail-modal` — the verification email renders as a **modal standing for your inbox**, deliberately OFF the design system (no tokens, Arial, an email client's idea of a page) because it previews another medium and will soon be a real email whose look must owe nothing to this surface. Its copy lives in `MAILS`, a template object one substitution away from the real transactional template.
- `alpha-flag` — **"Warning: docs.vote is in alpha!"**, fixed bottom-left of every page. Quiet by the palette's own rule — it asks nothing of you, so no hot colour and no glyph. At `--t-small` rather than `--t-micro` (a warning nobody can read is decoration), and **`pointer-events: none`**, because it stands over the document's own margin and must never eat a click meant for the text. The `devswitch` sits above it (bottom 47px); live mode hides the stagehand and the flag stands alone. Deliberately **not** on the magic-link interstitial, which submits itself on arrival — a flag there would only flash.
- `constitution-section` — the document's first heading is **Constitution**, and the constitution is **document text with sections**: under 🎩 who founded it and ⏱️ when it was *constituted*, the opening run, then **Membership · Proposals · Decisions · Anonymity** (that order since Q871, 2026-08-26; it read Membership · Decisions · Anonymity · Proposals before), the hairline, and the charter under its own name — **the band table and the founding order are SURFACE §8**. Three rulings worth keeping in view: 🤝 stands above the members list, and since Q865 so do 💤 and 🥾 (*how people become members* and how they stop being one, before *who they are* — Q617, half reversed); 🪜 has no clause and no rail entry (Q512); 🍾's clause **is** the Proposals preamble (Y21; Ed, 2026-08-22) — the tab hangs on the sentence the press makes true, and the sentence is tense-aware: *When the session begins, members may propose changes to rules…* until the press, *Members may propose…* after it. The paragraph stands tab-less from the save, so it is readable all through the founding, and **gains** its tab at 🍾's turn rather than a new paragraph arriving. Retired with the merge: *The document began at [time]*, which the Founded line already carried to the minute. 🌍 offers two rungs (Public left the ladder 2026-08-22; the catalogue still holds it, Q527), foundership carries a read independent of it (§9.3), and **before 🌍 is decided the clause states the interim rule** — *Until the Founder decides, only members can see the document* (Q618).
  **The section headings carry no intro prose** — the sections are not big enough to need explanations, and the clauses ought to be self-explanatory.
  - Within a section, **one distinct paragraph per decision, its tab in the gutter to its left** (clause-tab geometry), and the open card **replaces its own paragraph**, taking its tab with it, so the tab you click does not move.
  - **A question the members answer stands as a task in the section it decides**: the rule and the question are one paragraph wearing one tab where the reader is asked, and a delegated setting's watching paragraph (⏳) is followed by a *Your answer* task paragraph via `ctx.tasksFor`. The rails group by the same section titles.
  - **The document reads identically to every reader — only their tasks differ.** The constitution's sentences are **third person**, never "you"; what varies is which tabs stand in a reader's gutter and which cards open. One sanctioned exception: in the members list **you** are always at the **top**, on your own line, wearing your ✋🖼️ tabs, with *(nobody else here yet)* under you until somebody arrives (Ed, 2026-08-21 — a membership starts as one person, and that person is reading this).
  - **A settled card's head is the rule, not the task's name**: `headFor` serves the paragraph's own sentence (`decisionLine`, gates included) as the clause at the head of the open card. A title survives only on open questions (a question is its title), on the membership (the list is the clause) and on 📄, whose settled card shows **the text the document started from** (`cs.text`, immutable once confirmed).
  - **The constitution opens with the title and link clauses**: *The document is titled "X".* / *The document lives at docs.vote/x.* — deviations appended by `holderLine`, the title clause wearing the full governance tab group (**🪶 the value tab in front, the power tabs beneath**). Post-save the title card opens at its own clause; at the birth the tab stands beside the big heading (`fitTitleCard` runs **only pre-save**). **🌍 Visibility is the penultimate clause of that opening run**, before the Founded line and out of the Anonymity section, its rail entry riding The Document group.
  - **The Founded line stands from the save and gains its moment at the press** (Q640, Ed 2026-08-22) — *Founded by [avatar] [name] 👑*, then *… at [time] on [date]*, the time stamped from `constitutedAtT`. What is unknown before the start is the **moment**, not the founding, and the same argument that makes an unnamed document read *Untitled* rather than a gap applies to the line: it states the result so far. The fixture's logical clock is based at load time so a stamp renders as today.
  - **And it is the clause ✒️ and 🛡️ hang their tabs on** (Q639 (a), Ed 2026-08-22: *these clauses only exist to give "Your Pen" and "Your Shield" news a home*). The two grants write **no sentence at all** — their retired clauses stated a count that `holderLine` already puts on every setting the power turns and that the ✒️ socket's tooltip already says in the second person, and it was the one form that went stale when a power was laid down. They could not move onto any *settings* clause, because every held-able setting wears ✒️ and 🛡️ for its **own** powers and a grant hung there would put two ✒️ tabs meaning different things in one stack; the Founded line carries none, sits in the opening run where the grants arrive, and already wears 👑, *the mark of one who holds either power* — so the crown stops being a tooltip and becomes the way in. Built as the 🪪 pattern: `foundedClause()` rides `ctx.clauseFor`, `headFor` returns null so the card head is **Your Pen** over the Founded sentence, `onFoundedLine` is the predicate, and `tasksFor` still enumerates the pair under 📍 (the rail and the group's note count from it) while `lead` draws them nowhere. **Verified 0.0px**: the glyph does not move when the card opens; the box grows its documented 8px to the left.
  - **The document begins with its own name**: under a hairline closing the constitutional parts, the title stands again as the charter's heading with the 📄 tab beside it, **left-aligned on the prose's own edge** (the topbar's `.doctitle` padding and auto-centring are an indent — keep them off `.doc .doctitle`), and joins the contents rail between the constitution's sections and the text's own headings.
  - **Headings fold**: session-view's `sectoggle` ▸, inline in the constitution's section headings and on an **overlay rail** beside the prose's headings — a button inside contenteditable would become harvested text — mirrored in the TOC. A folded section keeps its heading, and **a section never folds while it holds the open card**. **Clicking a heading's text folds it** (the Constitution heading folds the whole constitution, leaving the charter below); a drag-selection or shift-click is editing and survives, which is what keeps prose headings editable. lvl3 subsection headings don't fold yet (Q406).
  - **The constitution's links are real links**: `linkify` wraps docs.vote addresses in `.doclink` anchors — ink-coloured, underlined, the underline waking on hover — **applied after escaping**, in the decision paragraphs and card heads.
  - **Inviting and removing are their own cards**: ✉️ *Invite a Member* and ❌ *Remove a Member*, **doors rather than decisions** — always settled, standing in the 🪪 stack from the moment judging opens. A running invite/remove motion renders on its own door (`motionTargets` routes by payload kind); the 🪪 card keeps the register and the pre-start invitation box (which post-start survives only where the founder's drafting power is real). Removal offers no founder-direct path pending Q401.
  - Glyphs: title **🪶**, link **📍**. The retired ones (🔗, the drawn home plate) stay in the reserved-faces scan.
  - **A document with no name reads "Untitled"**, grey, in the heading and the topbar brand — and the lane asks *Give this document a title*. This **reverses** the same morning's blank-until-named ruling (*Untitled was helper text standing where the answer goes*), on Ed's own user-testing evidence the same evening: **he watched a tester type the document's title into the prose column underneath the empty heading.** The earlier argument was subtly wrong about which thing the heading is — the answer goes on the 🪶 card, and the heading is where the *result* appears, so "Untitled" is an honest statement of the result so far rather than a suggestion. A heading with nothing in it gives the eye nothing, and the eye goes looking for the nearest thing it can write in. **A blank or never-entered *member* name still renders as "Anonymous"** (§9.0c) — a name, not a gap; the two rules are about different objects and neither borrows from the other. An invitee who has not arrived is listed by address, since the email is how invitees are listed before a name exists.
  - A paragraph states the **document's** rule, never your own answer — blindness intact. The 🪪 card keeps the membership list as its clause via `ctx.clauseFor`.
- `answer-bodies` — the consent controls and per-question copy (`ANSWER`) in setup.js. A founder who is a member answers the questions they delegated **on their own surface**, as a **separate question card** born when the setting is delegated; it stays outstanding, and the Proposing gate waits, until answered (§9.0b). 🎩 decides whether answers are owed at all.
  - **Choosing and committing are two acts**: touching a consent control fills a *provisional* value (which is what wakes the ✓), and the ✓ commits it. **Only committed answers count anywhere** — tally, gates, resolution — because a blind question's answers feed a count everyone sees and resolve to maxima, so a stray click while exploring must not be an answer. Motion consent takes the same gesture.
  - **Postponing the close never lowers the threshold** (SPEC §4.3): it keeps its current value and rises more slowly to the same ceiling over the new remainder.
  - `consent-slider` — the control for stating a minimum, with **nothing preselected**: a suggested value is exactly the anchoring that blind collection exists to prevent. Greyed until touched, because a range control with no default still paints its thumb somewhere and would read as answered.
  - `privacy-ladder` — the disclosure choices ordered most-private-first, with the rungs *above* your answer **dimmed rather than hidden**, since *the most I will accept* only reads as a ladder if you can see what you are refusing.
  - `distribution-strip` — the published shape of what people asked for, without names.
  - **The membership is on the screen while you answer**: what a member is judging is not what number feels right but *how much of these particular people I need behind a change*.
- `watch-half` — what a card grows underneath its own body once the answer is somebody else's. While the question runs it may say only **how many have answered** — any value or running maximum would let the room read itself before it had finished, which is the whole reason the founding is blind. Once it closes there is nothing left to anchor, so it carries the `distribution-strip` and what the document took. **The same half on both surfaces**: neither the founder nor a member who has answered may see more of a blind question than the other.
- `identity-cards` — ✋ *your name* and 🖼️ *your picture*, two cards. **Not authorship** (§9.0c): a name is how you appear **in the room** — the membership, the presence row, beside your own wallet — where authorship is whether a name is attached to a **proposal**, which is sealed by default. A document showing fourteen named people and not one named candidate is the ordinary case.
  - **No invented photographs in a mockup** — the picker offers a ground for your initials, a drawn mark, or an emoji face (`FACE_EMOJI` in setup.js, stored as `'e'+emoji`), generic by design so they claim nothing about a real person. Real uploads are backlog (with trying the email for an associated picture).
  - **Skin tone**: six ✋ swatches (`FACE_TONES`/`faceToneRow`/`faceToned`), the Fitzpatrick modifier inserted **after the first code point** so ZWJ sequences tone correctly (👩🏽‍🦰). The selector is picker state, **never stored** — what is stored is the final toned emoji, so nobody's face depends on where the selector sits.
  - **Any emoji may be a face — except the furniture, and except somebody else's.** `RESERVED_EMOJI` (setup.js) is a scan of the surface files for every glyph the vocabulary uses, minus the offered faces (a member whose face is ✏️ would turn every wallet into a possible mention of them), with **tones and variation selectors stripped before the test** (✋🏽 is as reserved as ✋). **One emoji, one member, first come first served**: the takenness test is a page-installed hook (`setFaceTaken`, the `wirePicDrop` pattern), taken faces render disabled with "X got there first", **exact-match on the stored string** so 👩🏻 and 👩🏽 are both claimable.
- `clerk` — a founder who is not a drafter. 🎩 *Is the Founder a Member?* is a card: two radios (*Member — drafting too* / *Clerk — not drafting*) with what each role means under it, 🗑️✒️ as its commit row, **locked once the document begins** (§9.6a). Its tab stands in a tab stack with 🪪 on the Members paragraph, and the founder's row in the invitations list wears a plain chip like anybody's. **It is a real question since 2026-08-21** (Ed: *🎩 seems to be missing*): it used to be hard-coded *always answered — member by default*, which is exactly why it never appeared as a task. It stands between the register and your own row in the founding order.
  **And it records the act, not the value** (Q682, 2026-08-23, found by the `phase-ladder` on its first outing). `isMember` arrives with the creation, so it cannot say whether the question was ever put — and pre-🍾 the surface kept that fact in `S.seen` alone, which no reload rebuilds. Since 🎩 **blocks the founding order**, a founder who answered it and reloaded was asked again *and* had the whole constitution below it withheld again. `convenorRecord().membershipSet` folds from the `convenor-membership-set` events the log already carried, and rides the view payload so both paths read it. Two halves: `setConvenorMembership`'s no-op short circuit meant a founder who was already a member and answered *member* emitted nothing at all, so the act is now recorded even when the value does not move — **once**, so working the radios does not fill the log, and without churning a roster that did not change. Exactly the lesson `nameSet`/`pictureSet` taught at Q645, in the one place that ruling did not reach; hash chain untouched, golden log unedited.
  A clerk **has no wallet, casts no judgments, counts toward no quorum and answers nothing at the founding**; their name and picture are **optional** (an anonymous founder is ordinary — they simply set neither). After the start the founder's membership is ordinary membership: it lapses and leaves like anybody's, and the right to re-tick themselves dies with the start. The delegation option is worded *you among them, if you are a member*: in the ordinary case a founder is handing a decision to a room they are standing in, which is a different act from giving it away.
- `proposal-rate` — the ⏱️ **Proposal Rate** card, one decision, stating itself in one sentence: *Members start with x ✏️ each, up to a maximum of y. They get an additional ✏️ every z minutes. Successful ✏️s are refunded.* Never "the economy", never "tokens"; the **stake** is a flat 1 (SPEC §13). The composer carries the three numbers inside the sentence as inputs; the delegated founding question asks the fewest-to-start (most generous answer wins). **The drip runs on real minutes everywhere** — %-of-window pacing is retired, so moving the close touches nobody's wallet.
- `wallets` — **every power is an object you hold, kept where you can see it, spent by flying it.** Four currencies, four scarcity rules, one gesture: 🪶 `quill-wallet` (`#quill`), ✒️ `draft-wallet` (`#penwallet`), 🛡️ (`#shieldwallet`), ✏️ `propose-wallet` (`#wallet`), 🏛️ `consensus-wallet` (`#voicewallet`). **The table, the socket states, the hold ladder and the rules are SURFACE §7** (spec pass 2); grants persist in `localStorage['draft:grants:<slug>:<me>']` (`ACK_KEYS`, wider than `GRANT_KEYS`, which is the four that fly). Code gotchas that bite:
  - A grant says who gave it, in the office's name, **read from `MemberRecord.arrival` and `SettingState.powerFrom`** (Q524) — folded from events the log already carried, hash chain untouched — never inferred from who holds the roster.
- `🍾 Begin` — **the founder's explicit act of starting the document.** A task, committed with ✒️, **stating its whole batch on the card**: the founder lays down ✒️ and 🛡️ on the Text, members gain ✏️ on it, judging opens, the constituted-at time is stamped, the threshold ramp anchors, and §9.6a's free hand closes. The only place in the product where the whole of what starting means is written down. The batch is **not offered as a choice on the surface**, but the module must permit one. Its card carries the **readiness readout** — per-person and per-question, founder-only — which **informs and never blocks**: until 🍾, let the founder get on with founding. **Module and server built 2026-08-21**: `begin(t)` is the only thing that emits `constituted` (nothing constitutes itself any more); `readiness()` is the readout; `begin` refuses while **any delegated question is still collecting**, gate or not, and while any judge-gate is unsettled however it is held (§9.0b, §9.7.1 — judging needs the whole constitution), and names what it waits on. A question delegated to a room that cannot answer it would otherwise survive the start unresolvable, unreclaimable and unsettable, on an adapter fallback nobody chose — and a clerk who delegates 🤝 to an empty room would seal the document for ever (Q626, R-045). **Page half built the same day**: the `begin` gate card (`beginBody` — the batch, the per-question and per-person readout, the hold), `[data-begin]` → `begin`; ⏩ calls `cs.begin` (nothing constitutes itself any more).
- `the close` — **the clock closes the document; nobody presses anything.** The closing act happened when the close was set, so T=0 is the room's own decision executing: a **final adoption batch regardless of cooldown phase**, constitutional motions resolve kept (the 🏛️ returns), **a pending 👑 fails closed** (carried-but-unassented, into the backlog — no crown-lapse auto-pass, since lapse is absence and the close is everybody's deadline), a mid-typing draft survives unproposable, an outstanding invitation expires, and **every member and invitee is mailed a link to the record**. SPEC §4.6. **Mechanism built 2026-08-21 (Q467)**: engine `candidate-undecided` / `DocumentClosedError` / `closedAt`; constitution `closed`, `motion-kept-at-close`, `crown-failed-closed`, `invitation-expired`, `close-acknowledged` (`acknowledgeClose(t, member, comment)` is the signature; `closingSignatures()`); bridge closes engine-first and serves `closeRecord()`. **Server half built the same day**: the minute tick drives the bridge before the constitution; `acknowledge-close {comment}` is the signature; the view serves `record` once closed (authorship unsealed per the anonymity ladder — sealed reveals at the record, anonymous never) and `mustReturn` for the clock; every member and invitee is mailed *“[title]” has closed* once. **Page half built the same day**: the 🥂 card (`closing` gate, `closingBody`, the sign lane → `acknowledgeClose`, `launchFarewell`), the closed page (`.doc.closedpage`, `SESSION.setClosed` — no typing, no wallet; `closedBlocks()` appends *Backlog* as one paragraph per undecided race under a PAUSE tab keyed `U:<raceId>` and *Signatures*), `speakerHtml(why, title, who)` the revealed speaker, `?fixture=session&closed=1` the closed fixture. Residuals: signatures render as paragraphs rather than with the disc; `carriedButUnassented` is counted, not listed; the stagehand *settle the session* button is the query parameter for now.
  - `session-clock` — one plain line in the topbar centre saying where the document is in its life: empty before 🍾 and on a perpetual document; the countdown while windowed, at a fixed granularity ladder — **days beyond a week, hours inside one, 20-minute steps inside six hours, 10-minute steps inside the hour, never finer, never seconds**; "Frozen — 3 must return"; "Closed [date]" forever. Clicking it opens ⏰, so the clock is the door to moving the close. **Cold at every distance** — the last hours' urgency belongs to the questions; a ⏳ entry short of its floor says so in its teaser inside the final 24 hours (a count, never a direction).
  - **The topbar reads the document · the room · you**: left 🪶 and the title, middle pulse · clock · quorum, right your wallets and `me`. **The approval threshold is not topbar material** — an abstract number that means something only beside the decision it prices. It lives in the ✒️ clause, in a judgment card's own copy of what carrying takes now, and on a ✔'s record of the threshold it cleared; never as chrome.
  - `closing-card` 🥂 — 🍾's mirror: a news card pinned until OK, one per member on their own clock, stating its whole batch (final as of when, what adopted, what carried, names revealed per the anonymity ladder, record published). **OK on it signs the document**: a closing comment collected by the rationale mechanic is the signing rationale — blank is real, dissent as welcome as praise — landing in the closing section's signatures block. The **wallet farewell** fires from that OK: remaining ✏️s and the 🏛️ fly into the card, the pencil storm inverted; the founder's ✒️ leaves the same way.
  - **The closed page** is the stranger's door's opposite: same URL, every clause grey, every tab filed, the `sealed-speaker` in its revealed form, wallets and pulse gone. The document ends **bracketed**: constitution · charter · **the backlog as ranked document text**, each unresolved question a paragraph wearing the third filed mark — **undecided**, drawn grey in the ✔/✖ family as two bars at the tick and cross's stroke width in `currentColor` — its tab opening its record card, the signatures beneath. The record's front door is the foot of the document (seeds Q464).
  - Build order when Ed says go: the topbar rework in session-view (clock to the middle, threshold stat removed, quorum joining it — measurable, probe-safe), then the drawn PAUSE in cards.js (probe-gated like any cards.js change), then the 🥂 card and the closed-state fixture, which wants a "settle the session" stagehand button the way ⏩ settles the founding.
- `Text` — **there is no "starting text".** One setting, the document's text, holding a value and both powers like anything else; "starting" named the moment we were looking at it from, not a property of the setting. This retires SPEC §9.7's claim that the text sits outside the holder system and that holding it crowns nobody — **a founder who keeps ✒️ or 🛡️ on the Text is a 👑 by the same rule as anywhere else.** 🛡️ on the Text means an **adoption** waits on the founder's accept, which is assent over the drafting mechanism itself and the largest single power in the design; kept available for consistency. **Built in the module 2026-08-21 (Q440)**: `startingText` seeds a `SettingState` like any held-able setting; **the start lays both powers down** (the 🍾 batch, performed by `maybeConstitute` until 🍾 is an explicit act), so a drafting engine's default is that adoptions stand by themselves; the road to a held Text is a post-start `reserve` motion, after which `textAdoptionNeedsAssent()` / `openTextCrownQuestion()` route each adoption through a 👑 question.
- `the four verbs` — **🪶 founding · ✒️ drafting · ✏️ proposing · 🏛️ consensus** (wording open). Read as a ladder they are **who has to agree**: 🪶 nobody, because nothing exists yet; ✒️ nobody, because you hold the power; ✏️ enough of the room, at the approval threshold; 🏛️ everybody. A complete account of authority in the product in four glyphs. ✒️ is not "the founder's direct-change power", it is **drafting** — writing the thing — and *proposing* is what you do when you cannot draft. **The pre-start blind founding answers are a type of 🏛️**: the founding answer and the constitutional motion are one act in two eras, both blind while they run, both resolving by a rule no minority can be overridden by — maxima before the start, unanimity after. Which is why the verb cannot be *amending* (before the start nothing is amended, only set) and must be about **consensus**.
- `quill line` — **🪶 covers the acts performed before anybody in the world could reach the document**: solo acts, freely done, with no oversight. The line falls at **the save**, because that is when the outside world can first knock — a stranger can navigate to the URL, get a real page, and be refused; refused, but engaging. The pen is issued when the URL goes live, so the two glyphs meet exactly once, with no overlap and no gap. Same hand, different instrument, and the instrument marks when the founder became accountable to somebody.
- `stranger's door` — **there is no login screen.** Logging in is asking for your address and mailing you a link, which is the **📧 task** the founder answers at the birth and every applicant answers on the way in — so a stranger arrives at the three columns like everybody else, the document is there, and their rail holds one card. **Server half built 2026-08-21**: a seatless `GET /api/d/:slug/view` answers the stranger payload — `holding {kind, sentence}`, `textShape` (heading level and character count per block, never a word), `canRead`/`text` where 🌍 permits, the constitution's values/holders/powers, the founder's name and picture, `applyOpen`/`joinOpen`; no member names (Q508), no answers or counts, no motions; commands stay 401. **Page half built the same day**: `STRANGER_STUB` the fifth seat, `strangerAsView` reshaping the payload for the member page's predicates, `barsHtml` (one bar per block at real metrics, measured in the column's own font; `?bars=black`), `#holding` under the title, `STRCARDS` (📧 Log In / Apply for Membership), `liveLogin` gone.
  - **The door has no provisional layer**, so it prints what the module holds: it hydrates through the member surface's own mapping (`hydrateFromModule`) rather than a second copy, an undecided rule says who is deciding it instead of naming a value, and the shared read body takes its lockline from the caller (Q510 asks whether the member surface follows). **A seat that dies mid-session becomes the door** — the 4s poll moves the viewer there, as boot does.
  - Open: Q508 (the members list at the door), Q509 (joining by arrival under *open* — until then the card is a truthful **Log In** rather than a *Join* that mails nothing).
  - **Redacted rather than blank**: the title shows, and the document's shape shows as honest grey bars at real metrics — grey because *grey means nothing is being asked of you*, and a redacted paragraph asks nothing. (Black bars to be built as an experiment and looked at.)
  - **The rules are public while the text is private** — the constitution reads plainly, because a stranger deciding whether to knock needs to know how joining works. That makes the door the page you read before deciding to apply.
  - The holding sentence: *The founder [name] is deciding if you can see this document*. **An unnamed founder is "The founder", not "Anonymous"** — which makes §9.0c's Anonymous rule a rule about how a member appears **in the room**. At the birth there is nothing to redact and the line is *"The constitution is being drafted"*.
  - One screen with one changing sentence serves every later state: **who decided, what they decided, what you may do about it**. A stranger is the fifth seat in the dev dropdown.
  - **Before 🌍 is settled a document is members-only**, carried on the surface as an interim clause identical for every reader (*Until the members decide, the document is visible to members only*) — the by-deviation grammar would otherwise leave a stranger reading a rule the members cannot see.
- `session-view` — the default member surface: the charter, readable top to bottom, with every contested clause marked where it bites and the `needs-you-queue` beside it. Since stage 8 it is the charter half of the one page: machinery in `design/session.js`, fixture in `design/fixture-session.js`. **The marks alphabet, the rail's rules and the card kinds are SURFACE §6 and §9** (spec pass 2); what stays here is each zone's name, its one-line job, and the code gotchas.
  - `contents-rail` (left) — the charter's own headings, following the reader, adopted sections only; no title and no note. Each heading carries up to four lifecycle marks by `KEEP_ORDER`; filed marks are absent, never counted (M10). Hierarchy is a divider and two levels.
  - `needs-you-queue` (right) — a **margin index**: every entry stands beside its own clause in document order, so *where* an entry sits is never a claim about importance. Pinning, admission and the fit cap are M1–M5; the rail and the gutter share one comparator (M6).
  - `lifecycle mark` — one glyph per entry, the same alphabet in all three columns: SURFACE §6's table. **Code keys on the mark kind, never the character** (`MARK`, `DRAWN`, `KEEP_ORDER`, `STACK_ORDER` in cards.js and session.js; the checker asserts all four). **The decided marks are drawn, not emoji** — the tick and cross are two SVG paths on one stroke width in `currentColor`; ↻ is a character styled as drawn.
