The ramp's own drawn incline was retired on 2026-08-22 when 🪜 took its place, and the `.mkg.fill` branch it was the only user of went with it — a subject glyph is an emoji, and only the lifecycle family is drawn.
  - `yours` — a proposal of your own, ✏️ from the first keystroke to the seal: a full card while unproposed, one line once proposed, force-kept beside the flame and the open card (M3).
  - `salience-diagonal` — the card asking which of two open **questions** deserves more of the room's attention. Served, never offered, only once the document holds at least E live questions and only to a member with nothing else to judge (§8.3a); no progress bar; terminates after three in a row; **one rail entry at the earlier clause**, titled over four lines; no `sealed-speaker` on either question.
  - `suggestion-anchor` — **the prose carries no highlight**; all the lifecycle colour lives in the `clause-tab`. **The geometry stays** — `.anch` and the `clause-head`'s block keep their padding and negative margin, because the card-opening motion is measured against the two boxes being identical. A clause is not a button: clicking it puts a caret in it (Y18).
  - `clause-tab` — the object in the `chip-gutter`, 34×30, the only way into a decision card from the document. On an open card the tabs are literally its tabs and **the strip does not reorder**; the active tab **grows 8px out to the left, on `padding-left` as well as `width`, pushed out with a negative right margin** so the glyph does not move; it keeps its colour when active.
  - `tab-stack` — the pile a clause's tabs make when the card is closed: front is what most wants you (`STACK_ORDER`, deliberately not `KEEP_ORDER` — M11); slivers carry hue, not a count, inert, 42% mix, squared at the top; **fitted, not fixed** (`fitStacks`, residual Q308); the filed pile at the bottom of the open strip, newest first (M13). **Code gotcha**: hole rectangles in the wire-shadow clip must be **merged before being punched** — the clip is one `evenodd` path, and two overlapping holes XOR back to solid.
  - `decision card` — **the clause, opened.** It *replaces* its paragraph; the shape is a post and its replies: `clause-head` → `proposal-block` per candidate → commit row. Every kind and its commit row is SURFACE §9. Geometry: **opening** grows from exactly the height at which only the `clause-head` shows, the clause itself never moving (0px on every kind); **closing runs the card's whole box back onto its paragraph** — it must not stop at head height. The card's own mark closes it.
    - `clause-head` — the clause at document size and colour under an eyebrow, keeping the clause's own highlight and gutter mark: the washed block is given **the same box as a `.anch` paragraph — same negative margin, same padding** — so the mark moves 0px in both axes. It carries a `lane-bar` exactly when the judgment can keep the clause (quick, patch; never a race).
    - `proposal-block` — one candidate: its wording against the clause (`result-only`), its rationale behind a `sealed-speaker`, its `lane-bar`. `sealed-speaker` is **drawn, not a plain circle** — a bare disc reads as a bullet. `result-only` states the text as it would stand, additions green, nothing struck, with the marking floor and punctuation as its own token (K19–K20); *(all text removed)* is a pseudo-element (K21). `lane-bar`: the radio is the loudest thing in the lane; **a lane may carry what is about that lane and nothing else**.
    - `commit row` — Indifferent at the left, labelled never drawn; the ✓ at the right, greyed until something is chosen; ❄️ only on the 🔥 card or one already chilled; **every radio on a card lines up down its left edge** (SURFACE §9.1).
  - `deadlock-card` — what a ⚔️ race opens into: the clause at the head (reference only), everything in flight full width oldest first, then **the desk** — the `editing-card`'s own `laneBoxHtml` and commit row. The field is `result-only` **with the floor forced off**. No standings, and therefore no lock. Open: Q301, Q305, Q306.
  - `sealed record` — every entry opens one: the top of the ranking **is** the head; one list of the whole field with win probabilities as percentages; the incumbent in the list labelled *Previous text* / *the text that stood*; the threshold stated once in the field label; OK bottom right — **a word, not a glyph**.
  - `evidence-meter` — closeness-to-resolution as a magnitude, never a direction, on queue cards only: the wash's fill is the bar, **the ground derived from the bar's own colour by swapping its alpha**, the leading edge drawn a second time so it shows at the bottom of the scale.
  - `queue-wire` — the open judgment's run from its entry into the clause it refers to. **A patch is joined at its cards, not in the rail** — the cable lives in the `chip-gutter`, leaving each tab by its left side. **6px, opaque, exactly the colour of its own queue card** — the wash composited over white, painted on a white cable of the same weight. A 10px cap at the document end only. **The colour layer is painted at full strength inside a group carrying the alpha**, so nothing composites twice. **Its shadow is clipped so every card on the surface is punched out of it — two nested groups: clip on the outer, the shadow's offset on the inner**, since a `clip-path` resolves after the element's own transform. It follows its card's colour over the same 700ms as the washes, keyed by the judgment.
  - `section-toggle` — the fold triangle, mirrored in the contents rail and the document gutter.
- `race-card` — the judging surface: contested text, two candidates, A/B/indifferent/propose-C. In the UI it is the session-view's escalation state. **`design/race-card.html` is stale and must not be copied from** — it predates the whole current card grammar (yellow cards, per-candidate verdict buttons, "Option A/B" labels). Session-view is the only current word on card design; bringing race-card.html forward is a rebuild, and Q70 asks whether it should exist at all.
- `composer` — **not a surface**: you compose by editing the charter. The rules (always-on-typing, the editing card, draft sites, the proposal row, result-only, the lane controls) are SURFACE §9.2, K13–K26. Its parts and their code gotchas:
  - `always-on-typing` — every clause **and every heading** carries a caret and **every input into one is intercepted**: the keystroke opens the `editing-card` with that character already applied. The caret is the accent blue, and where it lands is announced only when the surface moved it — never on a click. Its shape is not settable (Q281). **Every `beforeinput` on the charter is refused, the host-caret case included** (Ed, 2026-08-22: *the founder can still write freely in a begun document*): a caret on the column itself — its whitespace, an empty charter, a select-all — picks no block, and that one branch fell through without `preventDefault`, so the browser edited the column in place with no card and no proposal, wiped by the next poll. `contenteditable` is a hint; the refusal is the lock. Two locks beside it from the same bug: the module's `begin` waits on a confirmed text (§9.0b — a start without one wedged the document, since the text cannot be confirmed after), and `#prose` ships `contenteditable="false"` and is turned on by a render, so a page that never renders fails closed. `npm run journey` asserts the begun state and types on the host. **An empty document has one clause, and it is empty** (Q649 (a), built 2026-08-23): §9.0b allows a confirmed-empty text, and `blocksOf` drops blank lines, so an empty charter had no block to put a caret in and nobody could ever propose its first paragraph. `blocksOf('')` now yields one empty block keyed `L0`, rendered `p.editable.blank` with a grey `data-placeholder` drawn out of the text flow (the rationale placeholder's manner, so the caret lands at offset 0) that promises typing only to somebody who may propose. **The engine holds an empty document as zero lines** (`splitLines('')` is `[]`), so that block's proposal is an insert at `[0, 0)`, not a replacement of line 0 — `LIVE_HOOKS.propose` clamps every hunk to the line count the engine actually holds. `npm run journey -- --empty-text` walks it; it failed at a 400 before the clamp.
  - `editing-card` — the same `decision card` shape: the clause at the head, your draft as the single reply, the rationale in the `sealed-speaker`'s own slot inside the same lifted box. **The rationale's placeholder is drawn out of the text flow** — as inline content it puts the caret after it. **The lane preserves spaces** (`pre-wrap`): it rewrites its own markup on every keystroke so the green marking can follow the caret.
  - `lane-controls` — **B**, *I*, and one `[]` toggle for markdown, top right of the editing box; not in the topbar, since nothing outside an open card can be edited. **A candidate's text *is* markdown**: rich mode serialises back through `htmlToMd`, and switching view **converts the caret offset**, because markdown mode counts the syntax characters. Inline only.
  - `draft-site` — a run of adjacent blocks replaced by one piece of text; one site is one candidate; a second site makes a patch in the making. **One draft at a time, because there is one caret** (Q266).
  - `propose-edit` — ✏️ on either lane of a decision card: start writing from *that* wording. `Propose something else` survives only on a deadlocked race.
  - `proposal-row` — 🗑️ at the very left throughout (cancel → withdraw), the commit at the very right (✏️ hold → ✏️ Submitted). **The flight is an arc, drawn fresh each time** (a quadratic bowed off the straight run, sampled into keyframes — `arcFrames`); let go and it runs backwards at four times the speed. **The edit in flight is drawn exactly once, both directions — render state (`walletGhost` / `walletShow`), never a poke at the DOM**, because the drip rebuilds the wallet every second. 🗑️ is an ordinary outline button, not a red one; Propose is blue, not green.
  - Not yet rebuilt: `the-briefing` (heat-panel, standings-panel, camp-map, why-digest, graveyard, bridge-bar) and `dominated-account` — the briefing as an escalation state unrolling above the lanes. **§3.5 permits it only where there is no live judgment left to contaminate**, so a cold-start edit gets no briefing at all.
- `motion` — a proposal to change a **setting** rather than a clause: the one act by which a settled document changes its own rules. **There is no new object on the surface for amending a constitution** — an ordinary motion is a decision card with a value where the prose would be, and a constitutional motion is the card that asked the question in the first place, live again. The routes, prices and settling rules are SPEC §9.6–9.7; the composer rules are SURFACE §9.2.
- `assembly-press` — how a constitutional motion is put: the ten-second hold on 🏛️ in which the members' avatars convene into a circle around it, so **the assembly is the progress meter** (SURFACE §7.2). Under reduced motion the seats appear without the spring but the stagger stays, because the ring is the meter.
- `gazette` — public feed of resolved outcomes; the chamber view is its ambient rendering.
- `bounty score` — resolvable disagreement × salience: the leverage a good draft would have on a deadlocked race. It does its ranking inside the `needs-you-queue`, where a ⚔️ race stands near the top of your own margin beside the clause it is about. (The public *bounty-board* tab is retired — the sidebar queue does its job.)
- `record-builder` — closing publication: final text + the record (rankings, camps, graveyard, care map, minority map, backlog, audit log).
- `spectator-api` — strictly-public engine-core projection (gazette, live candidates, deadlocked races, document): the only surface spectator views may consume (Q42, backlog).
- `spectator-commentary` — optional LLM commentating view for convention spectators, fed exclusively by the spectator-api; unlike the sim's omniscient `commentator`, it sees no private data (Q42, backlog).

**Tooling:**
- `phase-ladder` — **one dev control that walks a real document through its whole life**, a phase per press (Q674–Q678, built 2026-08-23). Six **rungs**: `birth` (no document) · `constitution` (saved, the cast arrived, the questions outstanding) · `ready` (everything answered, 🍾 waiting) · `session` (begun, twenty members, thirty proposals of every kind) · `closing` (⏰ at now+5min, the real clock counting down) · `closed` (signed, with a backlog). It exists because every one of those states was otherwise reachable only by living through it, and ⏩ drives the fixture, which never reaches `hydrateS`, never polls and never sends a command. Parts: `packages/server/src/dev-ladder.ts` (the rungs, the `constitution-shuffler`, the `dev-cast`), `dev-ladder-charter.ts` (the Hollow Oak charter and its thirty hand-authored rewrites, generated once from `sim-harness`'s `clubhouse.ts` — copied, not imported, so the server takes no dependency on a package that pulls the Anthropic SDK), the `ladder-bar` on the live page, and `npm run ladder` (`scripts/ladder-walk.mjs`).
  - **History is written, not waited for.** Both state machines assert only that timestamps are **non-decreasing**, so a document with a real past is built by writing that past: the birth lands at `now − 3h` and the drafting spreads forward in strides wide enough for the adoption metronome to beat. **The window is centred** (`now − 3h → now + 3h`), because the ramp runs from `constitutedAtT` to the ending — a trailing window sits at the ramp's ceiling, where nothing adopts and every race card reads as maximally close.
  - **Rung 3 stops one press short of 🍾** (Ed, Q678): the start is the most interesting transition on the surface and a stagehand that spends that press hides it. What the ladder *cannot* clear is the ✒️ grant — its OK lives in `localStorage`, and it is `blocksOrder`'s one blocking grant — so every fresh browser meets the pen first and the constitution below it cascades in on the OK. That is the founding order working, not a gap.
  - **The constitution is drawn from the catalogue itself** (Q676), so a setting added next month is randomised the day it is added — but **clamped to a liveable range**, because a 🌡️ of 100, a 👥 count above E and a ⏱️ grant of 0 are all perfectly legal and all dead. Two settings the ladder keeps rather than draws, since its own rungs are defined in terms of them: ⏰ (its clock) and 🤝 (whether an applicant exists at all). **The seed rides the slug** (`ladder-<seed36>`), so it is visible, reproducible, and needs no store of its own.
  - **None of it ships.** The module is reached solely through a **dynamic** `import()` inside a `DEV:`-labelled block: the label is the mechanism, and the dynamism is what stops esbuild resolving the module at all — a static import survives the drop, because esbuild cannot prove top-level initialisers pure and keeps them with no live reference. `scripts/build-server.mjs` greps its own artifact for the cast and the route path, since that failure would otherwise be silent, and `verify-deploy` asks both routes **with their real methods** (a 404 for a GET on a POST-only route is what a *present* route answers).
  - `ladder-bar` — the control, off the design system like 📬 and for the same reason. Deliberately **not** a re-enable of `.devswitch`, whose seats are page state (`S.viewer` changes rendering while every command still posts under the cookie, so you would drive one member's hand from another's chair) and whose change handler calls `cs.arrive`, which `remoteCS` does not implement. Here a seat **is** a cookie: every control is a POST and a reload. Bottom-left, where the stagehand lives — the top-left stands on the contents rail.
- `card-audit` — **every decision card on every surface, opened and measured at once** (`design/tools/card-audit.mjs`, `npm run card-audit`; Q732–Q706, 2026-08-23). It exists because ten STYLE.md passes have each read whatever had just been built, and a chronological audit is structurally blind to the two things that matter most here: disagreement *between* cards (T5, T9, the §1 glyph table all quantify over the whole surface) and anything numeric (`.commitrow .btn { height: 2.5rem }`, the `--t-*` scale, the 4px grid, *every radio lines up down its left edge*, *one flat disabled look*). Six walks, 182 cards: founding · answers (`--delegate`) · settled (post-⏩, with the composers and the ✒️/🛡️ power tabs) · outsiders (member, applicant, stranger) · charter (`?fixture=session`, every `SUGGS` id) · closed. It borrows the founding drive and `snap()` from `founding-walk.mjs` and the motion stubs, the `toggle` walk and `rect()` from `session-probe.js`; it opens cards and writes JSON, and changes nothing.
  - **`--baseline=<payload>` is the second window size**, and it is not optional discipline. A geometry finding that moves with the viewport is a layout fact, not a defect — two did on the first outing (a charter card's automatic side margins, and the tab travel derived from them) and each read as a *different* defect at each size.
  - **Three boxes it must not measure**, each learned by measuring it and being wrong. **The tab is supposed to move**: the active tab grows 8px out to the left, so its rect reports the design as a defect on every card — the promise is that the *glyph* does not move (the 8px is on `padding-left` as well as `width`, so the content box stays 34px), so the glyph's content box is derived and compared. **A tab in a pile is not where its card is**: the tabs behind slide up leaving a sliver each, so only the front of a stack has a position beside a clause. **A charter clause is not surface copy**: the jargon scan reads `.headrule`/`.headtitle`, never `.rtext`, or the Hollow Oak charter's own *“Ordinary spending on the running of the house”* is a T15 breach.
  - **The rollup is the finding; the card is where it shows.** A stylesheet fact is one defect on twenty cards, and printing it twenty times buries the twenty-first that is only on one.
- `server` — `packages/server` (`@draft/server`): the thin product host. node:http, no framework. `npm run server` at the root. **Live at docs.vote since 2026-08-20** — one Render service, the alpha home, CI deploying on green and verifying the live host afterwards with `npm run verify` (PRODUCTION.md stage 10). **A push is a deploy.** Since 2026-08-20 23:40 the service is **stateless apart from Postgres** — no disk (498b), so deploys can hand over without stopping the old instance; the one nine-minute 502 was a health-check path changed in the same push as the route it pointed at — **land the route first**. Operator docs: `docs/OPERATING.md` (the map) and `docs/runbooks/` (the procedures — deploys/health/shutdown, the Postgres cutover, backups and the drill).
  - **Two stores, one seam** (stage 6): `Persistence` is the interface; `FilePersistence` is the JSONL layout, `PgPersistence` one row per log entry (`event` as `text`, never jsonb — it rejects NUL and lone surrogates). **The cutover is two variables**: `DATABASE_URL` is where Postgres is (inert alone); `DRAFT_STORE=pg` is the switch (set in production since the cutover), absent means `file`, and it never falls back silently. `dist/draft-tools.mjs` (import · export · verify · drill · repair-tail) moves the log between stores with **every rolling hash asserted identical** — the oracle is never relaxed, and no JSONL log is ever deleted. `pg` is the project's only runtime dependency, spent there alone.
  - **`/healthz`** says build · store · document count · uptime; one request line per response with the query string dropped (tokens travel there); **SIGTERM drains every write chain before exiting** (stage 7), which is what makes frequent deploys safe.
  - **Text proposals ride the same whitelist** (stage 8): `propose-text {baseVersion, hunks, why}` and `withdraw-text {candidate}`, strictly shaped, refused before the document has begun; a stale base is a 400 carrying the engine's *patch targets version N; current is M*. The member view serves the document **as the engine holds it** (`text`, `textVersion`) and a blind projection of the text races — `clauses` (contested spans, candidates with hunks and rationale and a `mine` flag, my own `judged`/`shifted`), `mine`, `records` (the last fifty outcomes; `p`/`threshold` only where the event carried them), ten cards, the wallet — never standings, never an author, never anybody else's judgments (§3.5). **One cookie per document** (`draft_session_<docId>`; the legacy name is read for its own document only). **Faces are reserved server-side**: `faces.ts` mirrors setup.js's lists (asserted equal by test); one grapheme, pictographic, not furniture, not somebody else's — *Taken — X got there first.*
  - **One hash-chained JSONL log per document; loading is `replay`.** The log is the only persistence, and **it holds answers in plaintext, so the data dir is as sensitive as the room.**
  - Magic-link auth: creation *is* the §9.7a mail, an invitation link arrives you (§9.6a), revival is logging in. Stateless HMAC cookies.
  - **A command whitelist injects the authenticated actor — a body never names who acts** — with `view()` as the only member read. `tick()` each minute for lapse/freeze. Every historical slug routes.
  - Mail rides the fold: Resend via `RESEND_API_KEY`, else `data/outbox.jsonl` + console. **Reserved TLDs are refused at the mailer** (Q680): `.invalid`, `.test`, `.example`, `.localhost` provably cannot receive, and handing one to the provider is a guaranteed bounce — the expensive mistake on a young sending domain. The reserved *second-level* domains (example.com/net/org) are deliberately **not** in the set, since the server tests follow real magic links at `@example.org`. It is also the seam the `phase-ladder` is silenced through, and it has to be here rather than beside the document: `relay` runs on every fresh entry and the close mails everybody long after any build-time flag has died.
  - **One document must never stop the clock for the others** (Q679): the minute tick's per-document body is wrapped, because `main.ts` only logs the throw — so one document that cannot tick would silently freeze every document after it in insertion order, once a minute, for ever. The tick is the adoption metronome, the lapse clock and the close.
  - **The page it serves is live**: `/` is the §9.7a birth (the creation mail is real, pasted text rides the stash); `/d/:slug` renders the real document through the page's `remoteCS` shim over `view()`. **The engine rides every commit** (`engine-host.ts`: the bridge born at constitute, `engine.jsonl` + `bridge.json` beside the log, resumed by replay; ordinary motions race, `judge-race` in the whitelist, race cards and the wallet in the view).
  - **The dev inbox is on the screen**: without `RESEND_API_KEY`, `GET /api/dev/outbox` serves the outbox tail and the page grows an off-system 📬 button (live, birth and login) opening a modal of every mail with its magic link clickable. **In the production artifact the route does not exist at all** — the build drops its `DEV` label bodily, so no misconfiguration can serve magic links (PRODUCTION.md stage 3).
  - **The operator hears about every birth**: the §9.7a save mails `cfg.notifyEmail` the title, the founder's address and the document's URL — the one mail the server sends to somebody who is not a member, and the one that does **not** ride the fold, since a birth is a save rather than an event anybody in the room is owed. **Fired and forgotten: the save must never fail or wait on a mail.** `DRAFT_NOTIFY_EMAIL` overrides; empty switches it off.
  - Not in the slice (NOTES.md): chamber-public reads.
- `engine-bridge` — `packages/constitution/src/engine-bridge.ts`: marries a `ConstitutionSession` to an engine-core `Session`. `proposeText`/`withdrawText` carry text candidates straight to the engine with no constitution event (a text candidate has no cs twin; adoption is complete inside the engine and the view reads `engine.document()`). Ordinary set-motions race in the engine (stake from the engine wallet, rival values one race); adoption reports through `adjudicateOrdinaryMotion`; what then *stands* (applied, or parked behind a 👑) flows back by a **standing diff** in `sync(t)`, which is the ground shift, plus an engine `amend` for the threshold/ending/rate/quorum/authorship. Roster truth relays **one way**: arrive→add, lapse→suspend, return→resume, remove→remove.
  **Deliberately not exported from index.ts**, so the page bundle stays engine-free — the engine runs **at the server** (`packages/server/src/engine-host.ts`), born when the constitution settles, persisted beside the document log, resumed via `resume`/`state()`; the page judges through the race cards the view serves. Evidence walk: `npm run motions -w @draft/sim-harness`.
- `constitution` — **the §9 layer as a real package** (`packages/constitution`, `@draft/constitution`). Pure, dependency-free, browser-loadable: the 19-setting catalogue (SPEC §9.7.1 is its table, asserted by `spec-check`) with **typed values, never display strings**; the consent rule as maxima under per-setting protective orders; the blind founding with live-electorate resolution and ground shifts; owed OKs; constituted; motions on both routes (constitutional unanimity settled live; ordinary through the `adjudicateOrdinaryMotion` seam, whose real caller is the `engine-bridge`); the 👑 question with crown lapse and auto-pass; sign-out/freeze/lapse clocks via one host-called `tick(t)`; applications on all four rungs; hash-chained log with **bit-identical `replay`**.
  **A refused event never reaches the log** (Q679, both packages): the clock check lives in `emit`, *before* the append, not in `apply` after it. It used to be after, so a backwards timestamp left a validly-hashed entry in the chain that `verifyChain` accepted and the host's next persist wrote out — after which `replay` threw on it for ever and the document was quarantined at every boot, unrepairable. Reachable by an ordinary road: both closes stamp themselves at the **ending** rather than at t, and an ending may legally be moved to a time already past. `apply` keeps its own check, because `replay` calls it directly.
  **Blindness is the projection layer** — `view()` is the only sanctioned member read path. Answers ride events in plaintext (NOTES.md records why commit-reveal was rejected). Ships a pure-TS sha256 (node:crypto parity asserted, lone surrogates included); engine-core adopted its twin, so neither package needs node.
  **The browser bundle is committed**: `design/constitution.js`, esbuild IIFE `window.CONSTITUTION`, exact-pinned 0.28.2, config single-sourced in `bundle.config.mjs`, byte-freshness test. Rebuild with `npm run bundle`. `npm run founding -w @draft/sim-harness` walks it deterministically with a narrative log. Author calls in `packages/constitution/NOTES.md`.
- `sim-harness` — synthetic-participant simulator driving engine-core; parameter sweeps against throughput, stability, bridge rate, backlog quality. `npm run evidence -w @draft/sim-harness` runs the deferred-question studies (deterministic, no network); findings in `packages/sim-harness/REPORT-deferred-evidence.md`.
- `participant-api` — the blind-discipline surface (engine-core module) every participant speaks: cards, judge, submit, gazette, browse. Humans, sim personas and personal AIs are interchangeable behind it.
- `scripted-persona` / `llm-persona` — deterministic utility-model participants (regression + welfare ground truth) vs claude-haiku-4-5 participants (realism).
- `welfare-ratio` — sim metric: (achieved − incumbent) / (optimal − incumbent) summed utility over the roster; 1.0 = utilitarian-best text found.

## The two design-notes files, deleted 2026-08-23 (Q726)

`design/session-view.notes.md` (342 KB, 68 top-level sections) and `design/setup.notes.md`
(20 KB, 8) were the build diaries of the session view and the two setup surfaces — every
design ruling from 2026-08-14 to 2026-08-19, keyed to Ed's own numbered points, in the order it
happened. Spec pass 2 marked both **superseded** (Q587) and gave `SURFACE.md` precedence wherever
they disagreed, which left them holding no live rule and one live reason each.

**They are in git, whole, at `aba22fd`.** To read them:

```
git show aba22fd:design/session-view.notes.md
git show aba22fd:design/setup.notes.md
```

and to find a section without checking out 342 KB, `git show aba22fd:design/session-view.notes.md | grep "^#"`.
That index is deliberately not reproduced here: a 68-line table of contents for a deleted file is
exactly the accretion this pass exists to reverse, and one command is cheaper than carrying it for ever.

Where their material went. **Rules** — the marks alphabet, the rail, the card kinds, the commit row,
the composer, the founding order, the band, the wallets and the holds — are `SURFACE.md` §6–§9, which
is tabular and machine-checked where the notes were prose and checked by nobody. **Reasoning** that
survived into an operative rule is cited from those tables as `→ why:` lines, or from the glossary
above. **The rest is history**, and history is what a sha is for.

Two things were salvaged rather than left, being live and stated in no other document.

- **Nothing underneath the view** (Ed, 76) — the three columns are the whole page; no footer, no
  notes, no second region to scroll into, and what lies below the last line of the charter is empty
  scroll runway rather than content. Now a line of the `session-view` glossary entry.

- **The empty members list** (Q407a, Ed 2026-08-19). A membership of one renders a placeholder row —
  *(nobody else here yet)* — where the other members would stand: full width, muted, no avatar, since
  a face would claim a person who does not exist. It leaves with the first invitee. The surface rule
  is SURFACE §9's members row; the reason it was load-bearing is not, and is kept here.
  Two reasons, one visible and one geometric. A members list showing nobody while the room has a
  member reads as broken. And founder-alone, the you-row stood so high that its ✋🖼️📧 pile collided
  with the 🪪 pile above it — two stacks twelve pixels apart inside one 42px clause, under the Q308
  floor, where a pile cannot shrink below its 30px front tab; `fitBand` squeezed the peek to 0.0 and
  they still overlapped by 18px. The placeholder gave the you-row a row's clearance, and the piles
  then cleared by measurement (5px at the default peek).

  **The geometry left the placeholder on 2026-08-25** (Q753/Q757). When Ed moved *you* to the top of
  the list (2026-08-21) the clearance job moved with it, onto `.memrow.meRow { margin-top: 42px }` —
  and nobody re-asked what the placeholder was for once it had no spacer job left, so it stayed where
  it was and ended up dangling *below* your row: full width and muted, the shape of a caption on you
  rather than a statement about the list. It now stands above you, and the 42px sits on
  `.constsec .cpara .memlist` instead, which is what stops the two from double-counting into a 64px
  gap. Putting it on the list rather than on a row is also what makes the spacer job genuinely
  redundant rather than accidentally so: the clearance is now a fact about the block, true whichever
  row happens to lead it, so the placeholder can come and go on its sentence alone.

  It has to be `padding-top`, not `margin-top`, and that took a measurement to see. The list is the
  first in-flow child of a `.cpara` with no top border or padding — the 🪪 pile beside it is
  `position: absolute` — so a top *margin* collapses straight out to the paragraph and takes the pile
  down with it, leaving the clearance exactly where it was: measured at **−3px**, an overlap, from a
  rule that reads as if it adds 42. Padding does not collapse. Measured after, 🪪-pile-bottom to
  ✋-pile-top: **founder alone 35px** (the placeholder row stands between them), **founder with
  others 8px**, **a member reading a full room 3px** (four tabs in the head pile there, against two
  and three). A clerk has no you-row and so no second pile in the block at all; their five-tab
  Founded-line pile is 46px with 25px clear above it and 45.5px below.
  **Anything that changes the list's leading row still has to answer the geometry as well as the
  sentence** — it is just no longer the placeholder's answer to give.


## ⏱️'s *I set it* fills its own fields (2026-08-24, Q740)

Ed, 2026-08-23: *when you choose "I set it" on the proposal rate, it should have defaults — 4 to
start with, up to a maximum of 6, an additional every 10 minutes.* The rule is in `SURFACE.md` F6;
what is here is why it does not contradict the thing F6 has said since Q511.

**Why ⏱️ alone.** Every other founding card asks for a *choice* — a rung, a date, a number the
founder has a view about. ⏱️ asks for a **shape**: three numbers that only mean anything together,
whose relation (the maximum is not below the start, the drip is in minutes) is the whole of the
decision. A founder who has never run one of these has no view on 4 against 6 and no way to form
one from the card, and the card answered that with three empty boxes and a dark ✓ — the one place
on the surface where *no defaults* left somebody with nothing to work from rather than a clear
question. The other multi-field card, 🌡️'s ramp, is F6's standing exception for a related reason
(Q512: the ramp is part of what the threshold says). ⏱️ does **not** join it there, and that is the
point of the next paragraph.

**Why a clarification of F6 rather than an exception to it.** The test F6 states is about what a
card **arrives** as, and SPEC §9.0a says the same in the spec's words — a setting is born with its
value unset, nothing arrives answered. Under Ed's answer (1) that stays literally true: the fill
hangs on the *rung click*, so the card opens with three blank boxes and the founder has to pick
before anything appears. What the machine then writes is an answer to a question the founder has
just asked it. F6 already carries the converse half — *typing into a rung's own field is choosing
that rung* — and this is the other direction of the same identity between a rung and its fields.
It is also **complete** rather than partial, which is what makes it a rule and not a special case:
`.pick .inner` is `display: none` until the rung is on (`design/setup.css:148`), so ⏱️'s fields are
physically unreachable before the pick and F6's typing half can never fire first on this card. The
radio is the only entrance, so hanging the fill there covers every path in.

**Why the anchoring ban does not reach here.** The `consent-slider` has nothing preselected on the
stated principle that *a suggested value is exactly the anchoring blind collection exists to
prevent*. That ban is about **blind collection**: many people answering the same question without
seeing each other, where one number on the screen coordinates a room that must not read itself, and
the document then takes an extremum of answers that were never independent. None of that machinery
is present on the founder's half of ⏱️. One person has taken the decision, there is nobody to
coordinate with, and they can see the numbers, change them and commit. Anchoring a room is a defect
in a collection mechanism; anchoring one decider who can overwrite what they are anchored to is
called a starting point. The ban stays exactly where it is, on the member's answer card.

**The two guards, and what each is for.** *Only empty fields* is the one that keeps the fill from
being destructive: a founder who typed 12, wandered off to the delegate rung and came back keeps
their 12, because the fill has nothing to say about a field that already carries an answer. *Only
on the transition* is the subtler one: without it, re-clicking a rung that is already on would
refill a box the founder had just deliberately cleared — the machine quietly undoing an act, which
is the failure mode every no-defaults rule on this surface exists to prevent. Together they mean
the fill can only ever put numbers where there were none, and only in answer to a fresh pick.

**Silent, per Ed's answer (3).** No *suggested* note and no explanatory line. A note would be the
card apologising for numbers the founder can read and change; and the surface has one place where
it does announce a machine-chosen value — 📍's suggested address (F11), which says so because that
value is about to become *public and permanent* and the founder may never look at the field again.
Three numbers sitting in three visible boxes above a lit ✓ are neither.

**Scope, per Ed's answer (4).** The delegated fallbacks follow — `ANSTYPED.rate` widened a member's
one-number answer with a hard-coded `8` and `240`, which were literally the defaults Q511 forbade,
firing exactly where the founder had delegated ⏱️ and set no machinery, so the document took
numbers nobody gave. They read `RATE_START` now. ⏩'s `SEED` and the post-start motion composer keep
theirs: the first is fixture furniture that writes `S` directly and never meets the click handler,
and the second should show what stands today rather than the factory numbers. The engine's own
fallbacks are untouched, being engine tuning (R-040). That the three sets now disagree in three
ways is real, and is filed as Q741 rather than fixed under a ruling that did not ask for it.


## The guarded gotchas, lifted 2026-08-24 (Q736)

`CLAUDE.md`'s eviction rule says that a gotcha whose mistake is now caught by a named automated
guard is the guard's job rather than context's: what stops the mistake recurring is the red build,
not the paragraph. The operative file keeps one line — the failure, and the guard that catches it —
and the post-mortem comes here. These are the seven that were over the cap on the day the rule was
written: the hold release, the live-path journey walk, the reason a change carries, the pen as an
amendment, the two identity tasks, the suggested address, and the two ⏳ tabs a member was served
about powers they did not hold. Verbatim and in full, sub-bullets included, so that nothing evicted
from the operative file is only in git.

**CLAUDE.md is operative; this is the record.** Where the two disagree, CLAUDE.md wins.

- **A hold is released by letting go, and by nothing else** (Ed, 2026-08-22: *I cannot submit it even if I hold it — the pencil flies back*). SURFACE W16. The charter's ✏️ Propose was bound per button inside `renderDoc` and released on **`pointerleave`**, and that is two bugs wearing one coat. A render detaches the button under the pointer, so the browser fires a boundary event at the node it just removed; and `.holding`'s own `scale(0.97)` insets the hit box by **0.78px**, which can cancel a stationary press with no render involved at all — measured, and the reason the earlier poll-deferral fix could not work: the live walk shows the hold already dead 500 ms in, *before* any render. It now takes the ✒️ pen hold's shape — bound once on the document, released on `pointerup`/`pointercancel` only, sliding-off-to-cancel deliberately gone. What made the timer survivable already is that it commits through `act(id, …)`, which resolves the draft **by id, not by node**: a hold that outlives its own button still lands. `spec-check` asserts the release set; `npm run journey` drives the real thing and fails on the pre-fix page.
- **`npm run journey`** (`scripts/journey-walk.mjs`, against a running dev server) walks **founding → 🍾 → grants → caret → proposal** on the live path and exits non-zero if it stalls. It exists because the two probes and `founding-walk.mjs` all drive the fixture, which never reaches `hydrateS`, never polls and never sends a command — every live-only bug this project has hit was invisible to them. **Since 2026-08-22 it drives the propose hold for its full three seconds with a real mouse, with a render forced into the middle**, asserting at the wire (`propose-text` 2xx, the wallet one lighter) rather than on page state, where a proposed draft and an unproposed one both read as *mine*. The disclaimer it replaces — that the holds could not be exercised because the tab is backgrounded — confused an *animation* with a *pointer held down*: rAF never fires here so no flight can be judged, but `page.mouse.down()` holds as long as you like and the commit is a `setTimeout`. That confusion is why the hold bug survived a first fix. It fails on the pre-fix page at *propose-text null · wallet 5→5 · held false*. Two harness rules it encodes, both learned the hard way: **press with a real pointer** (a synthetic `.click()` does not choose a rung), and **scroll the control into view first** — a pointer cannot press what is off screen, which is what made the founding look stuck at 💤. Two assertions were added on 2026-08-22 after both of that day's birth bugs walked straight past it: **the rail immediately after the save must hold the pen alone**, and **no `/api/` response may be 4xx or 5xx** — the double send's 409 was invisible because the surface *recovered* from it, so the check belongs at the wire rather than at the outcome. The refusal net does not reproduce the double send headless (the second click needs a real collapse animation to still find `S.open` set), so it is a net for the future rather than the proof of that fix; the rail assertion is the one that bites.
- **A change carries a reason, and says who made it** (Q530, built 2026-08-22). The pen was the only route that changed a rule *without asking anybody* and the only one that left no reason behind. Now: **the founder writes one on the card**, and **everybody else is told what changed and reads it**. Which half you meet is which side of the act you stand on. Four things worth knowing.
  - **A first decision is not a change, and shows neither half.** The founder answering a question for the first time has changed nothing — there is no *from* — and a room told the founder has decided fifteen things nobody asked them about is noise. The test is the module's own `previousValue`, which is null until the pen changes a value that already stood. §9.6a says the same in the spec's words: before the start nothing is amended, only set.
  - **Nothing about the hash chain moved.** `previousValue` and `setWhy` **fold from `setting-set` events the log already carried**, the way `arrival` did in Q524; and the reason itself rides an **optional** `why?` on that event, which is hash-safe because `stableStringify` drops undefined keys — an event without one serialises exactly as it did before the field existed. The golden log passed unedited, which is the assertion of both.
  - **An ordinary setting owes an acknowledgement when it is *changed*, never when it is first made.** Constitutional settings owe one on any set, as they always have (*a rule you had no say in is a decision you are owed*); an ordinary one the founder simply made stays out of every rail, since nothing was asked and anybody may motion it. One the founder *changed* has undone something the room was living under, so it is news by the same argument.
  - **The module is the truth about what is owed, and the page now listens on both paths.** `hydrateS` only ever *added* to `S.okd`, so an acknowledgement given once could never be taken back — and a change re-owing an OK is the whole mechanism by which it announces itself. It is symmetric now, and `syncOwedOks` does the same for a local module, since the fixture never reaches `hydrateS`. It only ever removes: a change may re-open an acknowledgement, and nothing there may quietly grant one.
  - **The founder's rationale is not sealed** (Ed, 2026-08-22: *the rationale should have the founder's name and avatar on it, and 👑 so people know that it was a founder action, even if they have no name or avatar*). Every other rationale wears the featureless disc because authorship is sealed until the record (§3.4). A ✒️ act is attributed **by construction** — only the founder can perform it — so the disc would be a lie the first time it was drawn. `founderSpeaker` / `founderSpeakerLane` put the avatar where the disc goes, with the name and 👑 over the reason; the 👑 is what carries the sentence when there is neither, since an anonymous founder is ordinary.
  - Copy rule inside it: **a long value is shown, not narrated**. *from x to y* reads well for a rung, a percentage or a date and badly for ⏱️, whose value is three numbers in a sentence — the one number that moved is buried and the *to* is lost mid-clause. Past 32 characters the pair goes on two aligned `was`/`now` lines instead, where the eye finds the difference itself.
- **A unilateral rule change by the founder is an amendment, and is reported as one** (Ed, 2026-08-22, reframing Q530 mid-build). It gets no list of its own: it joins **the motions**, where every other amendment already lives, so the record renders it beside them without knowing it is different. `MotionRoute` gains **`'pen'`** — the four verbs' own ladder of who had to agree, minus 🪶, which is not an amendment because nothing exists yet to amend — and `RaisedRoute` is the narrower type for anything that *raises* one, since the pen is never raised: it opens and settles in the one act, so it is only ever seen `carried`, and every place that acts on a live motion tests `status === 'running'` and steps over it. **Folded, not emitted**: synthesised in the `setting-set` fold from the event the log already carried, with `amendedFrom(id)` beside it for the old value a motion payload never needs. Hash chain untouched; golden log unedited.
  - The dividend is the one Ed's framing predicted: **a clause now carries its own history whoever changed it**. `changeHalf` reads the amendment record rather than the setting's last-set fields, so the founder's pen and the membership's motion produce one line with one shape — and **who it names follows the route**, as it must: the pen is attributed by construction so the founder's name and face are on it, while a raised motion's mover is sealed until the record (§3.4), so that one is *the membership* and wears the featureless disc.
  - **The clause keeps it permanently** (Ed's answer, 2026-08-22). Two hoists made that work: `changeHalf` and `lastAmendment` are **function declarations**, because a member's settled card post-start renders through the composer's *earlier* return — as arrow consts the history was drawn on the news card and vanished the moment the OK turned it back into a composer. Above the fold it is news; once acknowledged the same block wears a **Last amended [date]** eyebrow, because a line a reader meets months later has to date itself or it reads as something that has just happened.
  - **The record's own Amendments section** (`amendmentBlocks`, before Backlog on the closed page): every carried change in order, one paragraph each, naming the **office** rather than the person — the pen is the Founder's by construction, a raised motion is the membership's act, and neither sentence has to reach into the anonymity ladder to know what it may say. An **absolute** date, never `whenOf`'s relative one: *today, 11:28* is false by morning and the record is read long after.
- **✋ and 🖼️ are answered, and null is not the answer to *were you asked*** (Ed's live walk, 2026-08-22, Q645: *I'm never offered the "Your Name" and "Your Picture" tasks*). The same lesson as 🎩 above, in its sharpest form. `ready()` is permanently true for both — neither is in `CHOSEN`, and `myname.ready` is forced `() => true` because a task that cannot be committed is a task nothing can get past — so `S.seen` was the **whole** of `settled()` for them, and `hydrateS` could not rebuild it, so it simply declared both seen on every live render. Steps 9 and 10 of the founding order were dead on every real document while working perfectly in the fixture, which is why **no probe and no golden walk could ever have caught it**. What blocked the honest version is real: a blank name is a real answer — §9.0c shows it as **Anonymous**, *a name, not a gap* — and a picture is removed by choosing initials, so the value cannot say whether the question was put. The module therefore records **the act**: `nameSet`/`pictureSet` fold from the `identity-set` events the log already carries (`!== undefined`, never truthiness), the way `arrival` (Q524) and `previousValue` (Q530) do — no new event, no envelope change, **golden log unedited**. Two dividends: it is **per seat** by construction, where `S.seen` is page-global — the one "have you dealt with this" store that was not, so one seat's committed ✋ used to read as settled for everybody — and it survives a reload. ⏩ must answer both (a **null** picture is the answer *my initials*), or *settle the founding* leaves 🖼️ standing and the setup-probe diffs. Guarded live by `npm run journey`, which asserts the rail after 🎩 is exactly `myname, mypic` — **position, not presence**, since arriving in the wrong place is its own failure. Related, and fixed with it: **🎩 decides where the founder sits, not who they are** (Q646) — `convenor-membership-set` rebuilt the record from `freshMember` on every tick, wiping name, picture and both OK sets, and it carries **both** ways, since while the founder is a member their identity lives on the `MemberRecord` and the convenor struct goes stale.
- **The address is the machine's until you touch it, and the machine says when it picks** (Q534 (c), Ed 2026-08-22). 📍 is born pre-filled with `slugify(title)`, so on a collision the ordinary birth would open a task that is *already refused* — correct, but an unpleasantness at the third step of a founding. A **suggested** address moves itself to the nearest free one before the founder ever meets it; a **typed** one never moves, because there the address is theirs and a collision is a refusal to read and correct. `S.slugAuto` is the whole distinction: set in `fillSuggestedSlug` (the one place a suggestion is applied — it was three), cleared by the one act that can make the address yours. The move **says so**, naming both addresses — *Suggested from the title — docs.vote/charter is taken, so this one is docs.vote/charter-2* — since silently handing somebody a suffixed address is the machine deciding without telling them. Two guards worth keeping: the address it moves **to** is asked about in its own right on the next render rather than trusted from the response that offered it, and `autoSlugMoves` caps the chain at three so a server that keeps offering an address it then refuses cannot become a request storm. **`npm run slug-walk`** (`scripts/slug-walk.mjs`, against a running server, with one address reserved first) walks both halves; it fails on the pre-fix page at *the commit is dark on a typed address that is taken*. It exists because `journey` births with a unique title and so can never meet a collision, and both probes drive the fixture, where the check is synchronous and the live path is never reached.
- **A grant is the holder's, and nothing hid them** (SURFACE E8's audience column; found and fixed with Q639). A **member** was served two ⏳ tabs titled *Your Pen* and *Your Shield*, about powers they do not hold, cannot acknowledge and were never asked about — invisible while the clauses stood, since the tab had a sentence beside it. `hide: () => !amFounder()`, the seam 🍾 already uses; `spec-check` asserts both predicates and that the retired sentences never come back.

## The glossary prose, lifted 2026-08-24 (Q737)

`CLAUDE.md`'s entry rule says that every glossary bullet, at any depth, names something, declares
its kind and fits in one line. This is what the fourth extraction moved out to make that true:
forty-seven bullets that named nothing — prose sub-bullets grown eight deep under
`constitution-section` and eight more under `server` — and thirty entries that named something and
then argued for it at up to a thousand characters. Every one of them is below verbatim, in the
order it stood in and at the indent it stood at, so the entry each hung under is readable from the
indent alone.

Nothing here was deleted. Where a bullet was a **rule**, the operative file now carries the §
pointer and the rule's own file carries the rule; where it was a **reason**, this section is where
it lives now; where it was a **post-mortem**, it is in `CLAUDE.md`'s own *Gotchas*. A good many
were none of those three but a **name** with no name written on it — a part of the system described
in prose under a parent entry — and those were promoted to entries of their own rather than moved,
which is why `remoteCS`, `blocksOf`, `layoutQueue`, `standsOf`, `founderDirect`, `sectoggle`,
`linkify`, `pictureBody`, `emojiface`, `FACE_TONES`, `RESERVED_EMOJI`, `closedBlocks`,
`hydrateFromModule`, `Persistence`, `replay`, `view` and `notifyEmail` appear in the glossary now
under names they always had in the source. Eight had no name anywhere and were given one:
`provisional layer`, `dev-dropdown`, `door-cards`, `topbar`, `magic-link`, `mail-fold`,
`engine-host` and `dev-outbox`. That follows the entry rule's own logic — a bullet that names
nothing is one of the other three things *unless* it really is a part, in which case what it was
missing was the name.

**CLAUDE.md is operative; this is the record.** Where the two disagree, CLAUDE.md wins.

- `cards.js` [file] — **the decision-card grammar, one implementation** (`design/cards.js`, `window.CARDS`): a pure layer (the diff/markdown engine, `resultOnly`, `speakerHtml`, `fieldHtml`, `fieldOf`, the drawn TICK/CROSS and MARK glyphs) and a `make(env)` factory (lane bars, proposal blocks, `clauseHeadHtml`, commit row, `laneBoxHtml`, the open/close geometry, `keepStill`). Card kinds and their commit rows: SURFACE §9. Seams: `o.marks`/`o.washAttrs` on the clause head, `valAttr` (session radios speak `data-v`, setup composers `data-mval`, motion judging `data-motion`), a bindable speaker tooltip.
- `lifecycle palette` [concept] — three hues held as raw RGB channels: `--lc-open` 🔥💡⚔️ yellow, `--lc-weigh` 🌶️ pink, `--lc-yours` ✏️ blue (the accent), and one grey shared by `--lc-deciding` ⏳ and `--lc-closed` ✔✖↻. Two rules govern it — **grey means nothing is being asked of you**, and **hot for actions, cold for information**. 🔥 takes a fixed alpha clear of the ramp's ceiling (`FLAME_A`, 0.44) with the urgency ramp underneath, because 🔥 names a category and a ramp cannot express one. `--ok`'s green never leaves a card interior (Q264). The marks themselves: SURFACE §6.
- `type scale` [concept] — `--t-lead / --t-body / --t-ui / --t-small / --t-cap / --t-micro`, and **every piece of text on the surface is on it**. One exception is allowed to be a literal: a glyph size, which tunes a silhouette to a box rather than text to a scale. `.eyebrow` carries the single upper-case label treatment at `--t-micro` and **names its users in its own selector**; a shared rule nothing points at is not a shared rule.
- `spacing scale` [concept] — `--s1`–`--s5` on a 4px grid; radii `--r-sm / --r-md / --r-lg`; elevation `--shadow-sm/md/lg/xl`, neutral and never tinted — a drop shadow rather than a glow, each step a 1px contact shadow plus one soft cast whose blur stays near 1.6× its offset. Layout constants `--rail-left / --rail-right / --nav-h`. **Open is said by depth, not by outline**: no active box carries a border, and `--shadow-xl` is the only step allowed to be big.
- `session-view.html` [file] — **the one surface** (`design/session-view.html`, stage 8, Q418 (a)): one page from blank arrival through the founding to a live, drafted document. `setup.html`, `document-creation.html` and `founding-ceremony.html` are redirects.
  - **Three addresses, one page**: served from the product server it detects its address — `/` is the birth, `/d/:slug` a real document through `remoteCS` (a `ConstitutionSession`-lookalike over the blind `view()`: reads from the projection, commands as serialized POSTs folded under the cookie's identity, values re-hydrated on a 4s poll **but never under the open card**, dev chrome hidden). Opened as a file or from the static design server it is the fixture — no query is the founding fixture, `?fixture=session` the Hollow Oak session (`&band=1` shows the composition, `&closed=1` the closed page).
  - **One block is one engine line**: `blocksOf(text)` keys each line `L<i>` (a `# ` prefix is a heading), so a proposal on a paragraph is exactly one hunk and no mapping layer stands between the page and the engine's footprint. An empty document has one empty block keyed `L0`, and the engine holds it as zero lines, so its proposal is an insert at `[0, 0)` (Q649 (a)).
  - **It renders over `@draft/constitution`** — a `ConstitutionSession` born at the save (§9.7a) holds settled values, delegations, blind counts, resolutions, gates, motions and the register — and keeps rendering, its copy, and the **provisional layer** only: radio positions, unsent answers, a motion being typed. The `S` fields are exactly that and nothing more.
  - **One rail, one wire layer, one TOC, one open card**: setup tasks are SESSION entries (`env.extraEntries`) laid out by `layoutQueue` beside their own band paragraphs; the constitution leads the contents rail; band and charter arbitrate the open card both ways. Live, `itemsFromView(v)` builds the items from the blind view and `LIVE_HOOKS` post `judge-race` / `propose-text` / `withdraw-text`.
  - **Motions gate on `canJudge()`** (§9.6a — the founder re-sets freely before the start, so the composer appears only once the constitution is settled), and **what stands on a delegated setting is derived live from the room's answers** (`takenOf`/`standsOf`), never the founder's field, which only says who holds it.
  - The founding order, the band and the birth's own layout: **SURFACE §8**. The card kinds and the composer: **SURFACE §9**. The marks and the rail: **SURFACE §6**. The wallets: **SURFACE §7**.
  - **Who you are is the dev dropdown**, top-left under the topbar — deliberately off the design system (dashed, monospace: a stagehand, not a member of the cast). Per-user state lives on the roster rows; `S.myAns`/`S.okd`/`S.draft`/`S.mAns`/`S.myname…` are property accessors delegating to whoever is in the chair. Beside it, **⏩ settle the founding** completes the founding in one press, and it keeps 🌍 with the founder so a constitutional 🛡️ card and the crown route stay reachable by hand.
  - **Contamination guard**: `design/tools/setup-probe.js` against `design/reference/session-view.html` (tag `refs-founded-2026-08-22`), 42 steps, no allowances after a fresh freeze.
  - Open: ordinary adjudication in the page is still a **dev seam** (all-judged majority). The real adjudicator is `engine-bridge`; an ordinary motion should be served as a pairwise race card, not an everyone-votes tally.
- `setup-queue` [concept] — the right-hand rail during the founding. The five states, the founding order (`ORDER`), what blocks and what hides are **SURFACE §6 and §8**, and the checker asserts the order table against `ORDER` and `SEC`. Seams worth knowing: `blocksOrder` in session-view.html is where the pen is the one blocking grant; `orderReady` consults nothing but `ORDER`; `ansDue` is F7's *unless nothing else is outstanding* exception; `CHOSEN`, per setting, is what keeps every commit dark until there is an answer; the old default set survives in exactly one place, ⏩'s `SEED`. Walked by `npm run founder-answers`. Residuals in Q350.
  - `card-morph` [concept] — **switching cards within one strip is a morph, not a teardown.** The box survives: its height glides from the old card's to the new card's while the body crossfades, and the head swaps in place, so the tab you clicked never blinks and never moves (0.0px on both axes). From anywhere else the old card closes into its own paragraph first, because there the document *would* jump by a card's height in one frame.
- `commit-row grammar` [concept] — discard, commit, and OK: **SURFACE §9.1**. Two code rules inside it: what-it-stands-as is **remembered, not derived** — one snapshot per card, living until that card commits, never one per opening, since provisional values survive a close; and a revert restores **only the keys the open card can write**, read off its own controls (`data-set`/`data-txt`/`data-num`/`data-ans`).
- `reserved` [concept] — the founder's two retained powers: **✒️ drafting** (changing the setting directly) and **🛡️ assent** (a member-carried change waits on the founder's accept). A philosophical sword and shield: one writes, one only refuses. Held and relinquished separately and one-way; a `reserve` motion restores either or both (Q394). The routes, the timing, the crown's lapse and the delegation rule are **SPEC §9.6–9.7**; the surface is SURFACE §9's power cards and 👑 question rows.
  - **👑** is the mark beside a person who holds either power anywhere; **📯** the mark of one who holds none. *Code still carries the older names `unilateral` and `assent`.*
  - Built in `@draft/constitution` (`SettingState.powers`, `relinquish`). The founder's direct hand on a card **is** the drafting power (`founderDirect`), so an assent-only setting renders the founder the member composer; the composer's carried-to-👑 note keys on the assent power (`crownWaits`). The register's pair lives inside the applications value (`reserved` / `reserved-unilateral` / `reserved-assent` / `members`).
  - Out of scope by Ed's word: reserving the text (Q387); individual delegation, foundership transfer, inheritance (Q388). Residuals in Q349.
- `governance-tabs` [concept] — **every held-able setting is a tab group**: the value's own tab in front, and beneath it two power tabs, ✒️ *Can the Founder Make Amendments at Will?* and 🛡️ *Does the Founder Have a Veto?* (Q615). Drawn with the existing pile machinery (`ctx.chipsFor`, setup.js's `para`); a power card's head is that power's rule as it stands (`powerHeadLine`), its body the give-up radios (`powerBody`), its commit `commitPower`. **Delegation is the state of holding neither power**, and since Q511 also an option on the decision card (`delegateRung`, rendered once for whatever card is open); pre-start, giving up the second power on a delegable setting emits `setting-delegated`, opening the blind founding question. **The constitution states governance by deviation under a preamble** — `holderLine` appends only the deviations, and a delegated clause says nothing. The copy rules are STYLE.md T6–T9; the card rows are SURFACE §9.
- `applicants` [concept] — a subsection of Membership: the people asking to be in the room. Hangs off the 🤝 card's join policy, four rungs; where application is allowed a non-member's rail holds one task that spawns four (📧 ✋ 🖼️ 📝), and a submitted application becomes an ordinary motion to grant membership, its own one-candidate race against the membership as it stands (a synthetic `admit:<id>` engine setting, which is what makes two applicants structurally unable to be raced against each other). **Member emails are unique** — one address, one member. **An empty application is a real application.** SPEC §9.7½; the applicant's five cards are SURFACE §9.
- `alpha-flag` [concept] — **"Warning: docs.vote is in alpha!"**, fixed bottom-left of every page. Quiet by the palette's own rule — it asks nothing of you, so no hot colour and no glyph — at `--t-small` rather than `--t-micro`, and `pointer-events: none`, because it stands over the document's own margin and must never eat a click meant for the text. Deliberately **not** on the magic-link interstitial, which submits itself on arrival.
- `constitution-section` [concept] — the document's first heading is **Constitution**, and the constitution is document text with sections. **The band table, the section order and the founding order are SURFACE §8**; the clause sentences and their person are STYLE.md §3 and SURFACE §9's `setting` row. What stays here:
  - **The section headings carry no intro prose** — the sections are not big enough to need explanations, and the clauses ought to be self-explanatory.
  - Within a section, **one distinct paragraph per decision, its tab in the gutter to its left**, and the open card **replaces its own paragraph**, taking its tab with it, so the tab you click does not move.
  - **The document reads identically to every reader — only their tasks differ.** One sanctioned exception: in the members list **you** are always at the top, on your own line, with *(nobody else here yet)* under you until somebody arrives.
  - **Headings fold**: `sectoggle` ▸, inline in the constitution's section headings and on an **overlay rail** beside the prose's headings — a button inside contenteditable would become harvested text — mirrored in the TOC. A section never folds while it holds the open card; clicking a heading's text folds it, while a drag-selection or shift-click is editing and survives. lvl3 subsections don't fold yet (Q406).
  - **The constitution's links are real links**: `linkify` wraps docs.vote addresses in `.doclink` anchors, **applied after escaping**.
  - **Inviting and removing are their own cards**: ✉️ *Invite a Member* and ❌ *Remove a Member*, **doors rather than decisions** — always settled, standing in the 🪪 stack from the moment judging opens. Removal offers no founder-direct path pending Q401.
  - **A document with no name reads "Untitled"**, grey, in the heading and the topbar brand; a blank or never-entered *member* name renders as **Anonymous** (§9.0c). The two rules are about different objects and neither borrows from the other. An invitee who has not arrived is listed by address.
  - Glyphs: title **🪶**, link **📍**. The retired ones (🔗, the drawn home plate) stay in the reserved-faces scan.
  - **The membership is on the screen while you answer**: what a member is judging is not what number feels right but how much of these particular people they need behind a change.
  - Postponing the close never lowers the threshold: SPEC §4.3.
- `adoption-threshold` [symbol] — the confidence bar a challenger's win-probability must clear; ramps over the session window on wall clock. Formerly "θ"; always use this name. SPEC §4, §5.
- `watch-half` [concept] — what a card grows underneath its own body once the answer is somebody else's. While the question runs it may say only **how many have answered**; once it closes it carries the `distribution-strip` and what the document took. **The same half on both surfaces**: neither the founder nor a member who has answered may see more of a blind question than the other. SURFACE §9's `watching` row.
  - **A picture is an emoji, an uploaded image, or none** (Q734, §9.0c, R-046). The card is *pick an emoji* → *upload an image* → a *Currently* line with Remove; initials are not a section, being what the room shows when you have given nothing, so the card says so in a sentence. The grounds `c0`–`c5` and drawn marks `m0`–`m2` are **deleted, not un-offered** — `validPicture` refuses them too, and *no invented photographs in a mockup* is retired with them. `pictureBody` is parameterised and the applicant's 🖼️ uses it; it had rolled its own picker and had no uploader at all. **The upload downscales and re-encodes** — `createImageBitmap(file, { imageOrientation: 'from-image' })`, fitted into 256×256 on white, `toDataURL('image/jpeg', 0.8)` — and the orientation flag is load-bearing: the re-encode that strips EXIF strips the rotation with it, so a portrait photo lands on its side without it. `LIMITS.picture` is 40,000, set for what we now encode.
  - `emoji-data.js` [file] — **the picker is Unicode's own list** (Q732): `design/emoji-data.js`, 1906 glyphs in nine categories with their CLDR names, generated from the committed `design/emoji-test.txt` by `npm run emoji-data` (`scripts/emoji-data.mjs`), on the same discipline as the `design/constitution.js` bundle. Fully-qualified only; skin tones and the Component group dropped; a tonable flag per glyph read off the file rather than guessed, since People & Body also holds 🦴. **Only the open category is drawn** — the card body is rebuilt by every `render()` and the live page polls every 4s, so 1906 buttons is not an option. Category and query are picker state beside `FACE_TONE`, never stored, and the grid refreshes **in place** (`wireEmojiPicker`), because a page render would rebuild the search input under the caret. `FACE_EMOJI` survives as the **exemption list** and nothing else.
  - **An emoji is a glyph, not a disc** (Q732/Q735). `avHtml`'s emoji branch emits `.emojiface` — no box, no ground, `font-size: 1em` — so it takes the size of the text it stands in at every site at once; uploads and initials keep `.av` and keep the circle. This replaces a **specificity fix that could not work**: `span.av.emoji` is (0,2,1) and every contextual avatar rule is three classes, so each silently re-sized the emoji to a value chosen for two initial letters — 7px on the Founded line. Two places state a size, having no text around them to take one from: the navbar `me` (`.emojiface.face`, 20px on a 26px line — **the navbar's height is load-bearing**) and `#assembly .seat`. The picker grid and the *Currently* preview are `.emojiface.big`. Fixed with it: `.cpv.founded .av` overrides `display` to `inline-flex`, where `place-items`' justify half does nothing, so **the initials were miscentred too**. Measured by **`node design/tools/pic-measure.mjs`**, which walks a founding to a document with two emoji faces in it and reports each glyph's computed size against its parent's. **The emoji path is fixtured at last** — every fixture picture had been `''`, which is why no probe, walk or golden could have caught the 7px face; one member of the founding cast wears 🦉 and the setup-probe opens 🖼️ and searches it.
  - **Skin tone**: six ✋ swatches (`FACE_TONES`/`faceToneRow`/`faceToned`), the Fitzpatrick modifier inserted **after the first code point** so ZWJ sequences tone correctly (👩🏽‍🦰). The selector is picker state, **never stored**.
  - **Any emoji may be a face — except the furniture, and except somebody else's**, and since Q732 both refusals are met **in the grid**, greyed with their reason, because the whole of Unicode is offered and the surface's own marks are in it. `RESERVED_EMOJI` (setup.js) is a scan of the surface files for every glyph the vocabulary uses, minus the offered faces, with tones and variation selectors stripped before the test; 🍾 🥂 📨 joined it with Q734, closing Q632. One emoji, one member, first come first served, exact-match on the stored string; the takenness test is a page-installed hook (`setFaceTaken`). Mirrored server-side in `faces.ts`, asserted equal by test.
- `clerk` [concept] — a founder who is not a drafter. 🎩 *Is the Founder a Member?* is the card, locked once the document begins (§9.6a). A clerk has no wallet, casts no judgments, counts toward no quorum and answers nothing at the founding; their name and picture are optional, an anonymous founder being ordinary. After the start the founder's membership is ordinary membership. SPEC §9.6a; SURFACE §8 `ORDER` row 8.
- `proposal-rate` [concept] — the ⏱️ card, one decision, stating itself in one sentence: *Members start with x ✏️ each, up to a maximum of y. They get an additional ✏️ every z minutes. Successful ✏️s are refunded.* Never "the economy", never "tokens"; the stake is a flat 1 (SPEC §13). **The drip runs on real minutes everywhere** — %-of-window pacing is retired, so moving the close touches nobody's wallet.
- `wallets` [concept] — **every power is an object you hold, kept where you can see it, spent by flying it.** Five sockets: 🪶 `quill-wallet` (`#quill`), ✒️ `draft-wallet` (`#penwallet`), 🛡️ (`#shieldwallet`), ✏️ `propose-wallet` (`#wallet`), 🏛️ `consensus-wallet` (`#voicewallet`). **The table, the socket states, the hold ladder and the rules are SURFACE §7**; grants persist in `localStorage['draft:grants:<slug>:<me>']` (`ACK_KEYS`, wider than `GRANT_KEYS`, which is the four that fly).
- `🍾 Begin` [concept] — **the founder's explicit act of starting the document**, and the only place in the product where the whole of what starting means is written down: the founder lays down ✒️ and 🛡️ on the Text, members gain ✏️ on it, judging opens, the constituted-at time is stamped, the threshold ramp anchors, and §9.6a's free hand closes. `begin(t)` in `@draft/constitution` is the only thing that emits `constituted`; `readiness()` is the readout; `begin` refuses while any delegated question is still collecting and while any judge-gate is unsettled (§9.0b, §9.7.1; Q626, R-045). The card: SURFACE §9 and F5.
- `the close` [concept] — **the clock closes the document; nobody presses anything.** The closing act happened when the close was set, so T=0 is the room's own decision executing. SPEC §4.6. Mechanism in engine-core (`candidate-undecided`, `DocumentClosedError`, `closedAt`) and `@draft/constitution` (`closed`, `motion-kept-at-close`, `crown-failed-closed`, `invitation-expired`, `acknowledgeClose(t, member, comment)`, `closingSignatures()`); the bridge closes engine-first and serves `closeRecord()`. Residuals: signatures render as paragraphs rather than with the disc; `carriedButUnassented` is counted, not listed.
  - `session-clock` [concept] — one plain line in the topbar centre saying where the document is in its life: empty before 🍾 and on a perpetual document; the countdown while windowed, at a fixed granularity ladder — days beyond a week, hours inside one, 20-minute steps inside six hours, 10-minute steps inside the hour, never finer, never seconds; "Frozen — 3 must return"; "Closed [date]" forever. Clicking it opens ⏰. **Cold at every distance** — the last hours' urgency belongs to the questions.
  - **The topbar reads the document · the room · you**: left 🪶 and the title, middle pulse · clock · quorum, right your wallets and `me`. **The approval threshold is not topbar material** — an abstract number that means something only beside the decision it prices.
  - **The closed page** is the stranger's door's opposite: same URL, every clause grey, every tab filed, the `sealed-speaker` revealed, wallets and pulse gone. The document ends bracketed — constitution · charter · **the backlog as ranked document text**, each unresolved question a paragraph wearing ⏸, the signatures beneath. `.doc.closedpage`, `SESSION.setClosed`, `closedBlocks()`; the fixture is `?fixture=session&closed=1`. The record's front door is the foot of the document (seeds Q464).
- `Text` [concept] — **there is no "starting text".** One setting, the document's text, holding a value and both powers like anything else; "starting" named the moment we were looking at it from, not a property of the setting. A founder who keeps ✒️ or 🛡️ on the Text is a 👑 by the same rule as anywhere else, and 🛡️ on the Text means an **adoption** waits on their accept. In the module: `startingText` seeds a `SettingState`, the start lays both powers down, and the road back is a post-start `reserve` motion (`textAdoptionNeedsAssent()` / `openTextCrownQuestion()`).
- `the four verbs` [concept] — **🪶 founding · ✒️ drafting · ✏️ proposing · 🏛️ consensus.** Read as a ladder they are **who has to agree**: nobody because nothing exists yet; nobody because you hold the power; enough of the room, at the threshold; everybody. A complete account of authority in the product in four glyphs, and the pre-start blind founding answers are a type of 🏛️. SURFACE §7's table is the ladder in full.
- `stranger's door` [concept] — **there is no login screen.** A stranger arrives at the three columns like everybody else, the document is there, and their rail holds one card. Server half: a seatless `GET /api/d/:slug/view` answering `holding {kind, sentence}`, `textShape` (heading level and character count per block, never a word), `canRead`/`text` where 🌍 permits, the constitution's values and holders, the founder's name and picture, `applyOpen`/`joinOpen`; no member names (Q508), no answers or counts, no motions, commands 401. Page half: `STRANGER_STUB` the fifth seat, `strangerAsView`, `barsHtml`, `#holding` under the title, `STRCARDS`.
  - **The door has no provisional layer**, so it prints what the module holds: it hydrates through the member surface's own mapping (`hydrateFromModule`) rather than a second copy, and an undecided rule says who is deciding it instead of naming a value (Q510 asks whether the member surface follows). **A seat that dies mid-session becomes the door.**
  - **Redacted rather than blank**, and **the rules are public while the text is private**: the title shows, the document's shape shows as grey bars at real metrics, and the constitution reads plainly, because a stranger deciding whether to knock needs to know how joining works. One screen with one changing sentence serves every later state — *who decided, what they decided, what you may do about it*. **An unnamed founder is "The founder", not "Anonymous"**, which makes §9.0c's rule one about how a member appears in the room.
  - Open: Q508 (the members list at the door), Q509 (joining by arrival under *open*).
- `session-view` [concept] — the default member surface: the charter, readable top to bottom, with every contested clause marked where it bites and the `needs-you-queue` beside it. Machinery in `design/session.js`, fixture in `design/fixture-session.js`. **The marks alphabet, the rail's rules and the card kinds are SURFACE §6 and §9**; what stays here is each zone's name and its one-line job.
  - **Nothing underneath the view** (Ed, 76). The three columns are the whole page: no footer, no notes, no second region to scroll into. What lies below the last line of the charter is empty scroll runway, not content.
  - `decision card` [concept] — **the clause, opened.** It *replaces* its paragraph; the shape is a post and its replies: `clause-head` → `proposal-block` per candidate → commit row. Every kind and its commit row is SURFACE §9. Geometry: opening grows from exactly the height at which only the `clause-head` shows, the clause itself never moving (0px on every kind); **closing runs the card's whole box back onto its paragraph** — it must not stop at head height.
  - `always-on-typing` [concept] — every clause **and every heading** carries a caret and **every input into one is intercepted**: the keystroke opens the `editing-card` with that character already applied. `contenteditable` is a hint; the refusal is the lock, and `#prose` ships `contenteditable="false"` and is turned on by a render, so a page that never renders fails closed. The caret's shape is not settable (Q281).
  - Not yet rebuilt: `the-briefing` (heat-panel, standings-panel, camp-map, why-digest, graveyard, bridge-bar) and `dominated-account` — the briefing as an escalation state unrolling above the lanes. **SPEC §3.5 permits it only where there is no live judgment left to contaminate.**
- `motion` [concept] — a proposal to change a **setting** rather than a clause: the one act by which a settled document changes its own rules. **There is no new object on the surface for amending a constitution.** The routes, prices and settling rules are SPEC §9.6–9.7; the composer rules are SURFACE §9.2. `MotionRoute` carries `'pen'` for a founder's unilateral change, which is an amendment and is reported as one; `RaisedRoute` is the narrower type for anything that *raises* one.
- `phase-ladder` [concept] — **one dev control that walks a real document through its whole life**, a phase per press (Q674–Q678). Six rungs: `birth` · `constitution` · `ready` (🍾 waiting) · `session` (twenty members, thirty proposals) · `closing` (⏰ at now+5min) · `closed`.
